# On-Policy Distillation (OPD) Survey: Paper Catalog

[中文](./On-Policy-Distillation-Literature-Review-Papers.md) | English

- **Research date**: 2026-11-02 (evidence cutoff: arXiv IDs up to 2610; the latest paper was submitted on 2026-10-30)
- **Navigation**: [main page](./On-Policy-Distillation-Literature-Review_en.md) (conclusions, mechanism analysis, OPSD, MOPD, OPD and RL, application domains, GitHub paper lists)
- **Scope**: of 704 candidate papers first submitted to arXiv in August–October 2026, 540 are core OPD papers and 136 are related work; the other 28 are irrelevant and not listed.
- **Basis**: the classification and the "What it does" column rely only on each paper's abstract and comments; the inclusion criteria are in Section 1 of the main page.

## How to read this catalog

- Core papers are grouped by their first method label and sorted by arXiv ID within each section. A paper may carry two labels but appears only in the section of its first label.
- ★ marks importance 3: an analysis that raises a new question or perspective, a general method with strong evidence, industrial practice, or the first paper in a new direction. Importance is a subjective rating.
- The "Teacher" column: External white-box = a larger teacher whose logits are available; Black-box = a teacher with only text outputs; Same model, privileged = OPSD; Own earlier checkpoint = the model's own earlier checkpoint; Multi-teacher = several expert teachers; Weak teacher = a teacher weaker than the student.
- The "Lists" column shows which GitHub lists include the paper. C = chrisliu298, T = thinkwee, N = nick7nlp, J = Jingchensun (multimodal), D = DjangoJungle (MOPD), H = HJSang (OPSD), K = kissshhot, X = hhlxm; "-" means none of them.
- "Result" quotes only numbers or conclusions stated explicitly in the abstract; it is omitted when the abstract gives none.
- Papers marked "withdrawn" have been withdrawn by their authors; do not cite their conclusions.

## Papers per section

- Analysis and theory: 71
- Objectives and signal design: 113
- Teacher-side improvements: 59
- Rollouts, data and efficiency: 28
- On-policy self-distillation (OPSD): 177
- Multi-teacher OPD (MOPD): 42
- Combining OPD and RL: 34
- Cross-tokenizer and cross-architecture: 16
- Related work: 136

## Analysis and theory (71)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [2608.04408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04408) (withdrawn) | Not Every Divergence Should Be Suppressed: Counterfactual Recoverability in On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Turns the question of whether a divergence should be suppressed into one of counterfactual recoverability: each error state is replayed through budget-matched teacher-continuation and rollback branches, which decides whether to retain, roll back or conventionally supervise the trajectory. Result: The recoverability proxy reaches AUC 1.000 versus 0.392 for divergence alone; AIME2025 success is 0.578 against 0.517 for the baseline. | C |
| ★[2608.04794](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04794) | Privileged, but Biased: How PI-Conditioned Teachers Break Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Systematically reproduces and then refutes the gains of privileged-information self-distillation on hard tasks, and gives a causal chain from PI bias to student degradation. Result: On hard tasks the per-token loss keeps falling while validation accuracy does not rise but instead degrades. | CTNX |
| ★[2608.06802](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06802) | Simple-OPD: Demystifying Warm-up for On-policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Dissects the warm-up stage before OPD from both the data and the training side, and proposes initializing with teacher CoT plus near-saturation LoRA training. Result: Warming up on incorrect teacher rollouts works about as well as on correct ones; LoRA beats full-parameter SFT. | CT |
| ★[2608.09228](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09228) | Privileged Solutions or Context-Induced Teacher Behavior? Dissecting On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Replaces the paired reference solution with the question and solution of another problem (OP²SD), leaving the rollouts, teacher and objective unchanged, so as to separate the privileged answer from the context effect. Result: OP²SD beats the base model and is comparable to OPSD on three models and three math benchmarks. | CTNH |
| ★[2608.09263](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09263) | Privileged Likelihood Is Not Automatically Value: Three Checks for Token Credit in On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Questions taking OPSD's token-level privileged likelihood as an action value: what it answers is how predictions change once the context becomes richer, and three checks test whether it is valid. Result: On AIME 2025 the score separates correct from incorrect near chance (AUC=0.505), and Avg@4 is far below GRPO's 64.2%. | CTN |
| ★[2608.11829](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11829) | Towards Understanding On-Policy Distillation through the Lens of Test-Time Scaling [code](https://github.com/Geraldxm/opd-test-time-scaling) | General LLM | External white-box | Revisits OPD through test-time scaling: varying the sampling budget and looking at pass@K shows that gains at a small budget may turn into drops at a large one, and proposes TAS@K, computable before training, for choosing a teacher. Result: Across thirteen benchmarks in three domains, the teacher ranking predicted by TAS@K agrees with the actual gains 83.6% of the time. | CTNX |
| [2608.15787](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15787) | Routing Divergence Is Not Evidence of Behavioral Influence in Same-Weight MoE Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | For same-weight MoE self-distillation where an example-conditioned teacher supervises a student that sees only the question, uses a single-step, frozen-weight blockwise decomposition to separate a routing term from a content term, and measures each one's residual-stream exposure and behavioral influence. Result: The routing term's influence on outputs is less than half the natural context effect, and it can largely be reproduced by noise of the same norm. | C |
| ★[2608.16647](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16647) | Every Coin Has Two Sides: On the Dual Nature of Generalization in On-Policy Distillation of Large Language Models | General LLM | External white-box | A controlled study that varies one factor at a time: OPD transfers the teacher's reasoning behavior rather than specific answers, transfer strength depends on whether teacher and student share an origin, and this is used to diagnose the capability seesaw in multi-teacher OPD. Result: Training difficulty has almost no effect, and problems the teacher cannot solve are also useful; with a same-origin teacher, the student can approach the teacher across languages and domains. | CTD |
| ★[2608.18271](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18271) | Rethinking Privileged Information in On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Separates the supervision induced by the reference answer from that of a reference-free teacher to test whether OPSD gains really come from privileged reference information or from recovering reasoning behavior the base model already has. Result: A correct reference is not reliably helpful; using solutions to other problems is actually better on several math benchmarks. | CT |
| [2608.25643](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25643) | A Token-Level Analysis of Sampled-Token Reverse-KL On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Derives the per-token gradient of the K2 estimator of reverse KL, splits it into the teacher-student log-probability difference and a student-side softmax factor, and on that basis proposes SuRe, a bounded surprisal reweighting. Result: Tokens with low student probability take a disproportionate share of the gradient norm; SuRe improves several math metrics at two Qwen3 sizes. | CTN |
| ★[2608.25936](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25936) | One Symptom, Three Levers: A Critical Review of On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | A critical review of OPSD that treats collapse as a symptom governed by three levers (which tokens the signal is applied to, what privileged information the teacher gets, and how guidance decays over time), and unifies terminology across the literature. Result: Runs no new experiments, is limited in scope to math reasoning, and separates what is settled from what is still disputed. | CT |
| [2608.27409](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27409) | Consolidating RLVR Capabilities Across Domains: A Deep Dive into Fusion Paradigms | General LLM | Multi-teacher | Splits RLVR capability fusion by the reused artifact into three paradigms, Merge, Mix RL and multi-teacher OPD, compares them across scales with the same experts and data, and gives selection guidelines. Result: The three differ by at most 1.4 points on average but by up to 8.6 points on single benchmarks; all of them only improve single-sample accuracy, with no gain in solution coverage. | ND |
| ★[2608.29846](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29846) | Influence-Directed Distillation: Solving the Diversity Bottleneck in Sampled-Token On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Shows that sampled-token OPD raises pass@1 while pass@k stalls, decomposes the update by first-order local entropy influence, keeps the entropy-increasing terms, and shrinks the advantage of entropy-decreasing terms according to divergence. Result: Consistently improves pass@k, matches the strongest teacher-information method at lower cost, and largely preserves the original OPD's pass@1. | CN |
| ★[2608.31046](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.31046) | Does On-Policy Distillation Really Distill? From Noisy Teacher to Self-Improvement [code](https://github.com/DripNowhy/On-Policy-Self-Adaptation) | General LLM | External white-box | Quantifies that noise in OPD teacher supervision grows with teacher size while the student is insensitive to it, and that the gains come mainly from pushing down low-log-probability tokens, then proposes OPSA, a supervision-free, entropy-adaptive negative advantage. Result: OPSA's Avg@32 on AIME24 is 35.41 points above the base model and 16.77 points above OPD. | CTN |
| ★[2609.04108](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04108) | Sequential Beats Joint: On the Interplay between On-Policy Distillation and RLVR | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically compares joint and staged combinations of OPD and RLVR, argues that OPD followed by RL is better, and explains their complementarity through pass@k, learning dynamics and parameter updates. Result: OPD-then-RL reliably beats pure OPD, pure RLVR and various joint baselines; OPD is better suited than SFT as an RL cold start. | CNK |
| ★[2609.04172](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04172) | Rethinking On-Policy Distillation of Large Language Models II: One Training Example [code](https://github.com/Thinking-Space/One-Shot-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Studies OPD at the data limit of a single query, uses "state coverage" and the teacher-student alignment rate to explain why one example suffices, and argues that OPD has surplus data but is algorithm-limited. Result: A single query gives 71.5% state coverage, and 16 semantically different queries reach 98.9% and match full-data training. | CTNDKX |
| ★[2609.04565](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04565) | Extremely Sparse Supervision Incentivizes Reasoning Ability | General LLM | External white-box | In the OPD setting, finds that applying teacher supervision to only a handful of tokens in each reasoning trajectory is enough to elicit reasoning ability, challenging the assumption that post-training must be token-dense. Result: With only one or two tokens per trajectory (about 0.05%), it matches or exceeds all-token training in most cases, verified on nine teacher-student configurations. | N |
| [2609.05198](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05198) | What Matters in On-Policy Distillation? A Perspective on Data Efficiency and Data Selection | General LLM | External white-box | An empirical study of data efficiency and selection in OPD: 1-shot works broadly and harder problems work better, the gain comes from long CoT rather than high entropy, so only hard examples are selected for training. Result: Across five models from 1.5B to 8B, just 8 curated hard examples match the 17K-dataset baseline, and "unsolvable" samples are usable too. | CNKX |
| ★[2609.14193](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14193) | Data-Free On-Policy Distillation: How Far Can We Go Without External Data? [code](https://github.com/Ryuki661/DF-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically examines how much OPD depends on training questions, finds that very few prompts already expose enough teacher supervision, and proposes a data-free OPD setting in which the system generates its own questions. Result: 8 real prompts ≈ 17k problems; 64 self-generated questions match full OPD; with multiple teachers 1k generated questions ≈ 7k real post-training samples. | CTNDK |
| ★[2609.20511](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20511) | When EOS Tokens Disagree: Understanding Length Inflation in On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/UNCSciML/opd-eos) | General LLM | External white-box | Attributes the growing length of student answers in OPD to termination-token mismatch: the base student and the post-trained teacher put stop probability on different EOS tokens, so the student's preferred termination is suppressed without the teacher's preference being transferred. Result: The mismatch shows up in all three families Qwen3, Llama and Gemma; aligning the stop set alone is not enough, while treating them as a shared semantic stop action helps significantly. | CNK |
| ★[2609.20612](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20612) | What Does Privileged Information Add to On-Policy Self-Distillation? [code](https://github.com/xiuyuz/opsd-reference-study) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Builds the AMPLE-Math suite with six reasoning views of the same answer and compares each privileged view one by one against a matched reference-free distillation, isolating what privileged information adds beyond distillation itself. Result: Reference-free distillation already accounts for most of the gain on Qwen3-1.7B, and switching to long-thinking rollouts turns the gain into a loss. | CTH |
| [2609.21561](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21561) | On Repulsive and Attractive Teachers: Separating Correctness from Behavior in Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Privileged information changes both what the teacher knows and how it behaves; after comparing attractive and repulsive self-distillation, a contrastive self-distillation moves toward the teacher on correct solutions and away from it on incorrect ones to cancel the behavioral shift. Result: Attraction suppresses exploration and shortens answers, while repulsion lengthens them and can even trigger a latent thinking mode and is unstable; the contrastive objective improves reasoning with stable length on non-thinking, instruct and already-thinking models. | C |
| [2609.22109](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22109) | A Shared Learning Rate Is Not a Neutral Control in Selective On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Points out that a shared learning rate is not neutral in the comparison protocol of selective OPD: dense supervision is insensitive to the learning rate, while token selectors swing widely with it, enough to flip conclusions. Result: The dense swing of 1.8pp is not significant while the teachability selector reaches 17.7pp, and the dense-versus-selective conclusion differs by 2× between the two learning rates. | - |
| [2609.25048](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25048) | Teaching a Moving Student: Rethinking the Curriculum of On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Studies the OPD curriculum problem created by student policy drift: which of the current and initial policy rollouts is better flips with the generation budget, so replay of initial trajectories is triggered by gradient changes. Result: On eight math benchmarks, 0.6B/1.7B gain 2.25/4.44 and 2.81/6.15 points at 16K/32K. | CN |
| ★[2609.28145](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28145) | RL Starts before RL: On Policy Distillation for Better Reinforcement Learning | General LLM | External white-box | Treats OPD as preparation before RL: a student initialized by OPD ends up with better RL performance than direct RL and SFT+RL, and the advantage does not necessarily show in immediate accuracy. Result: Reverse KL is better before RL but is overtaken by forward KL after RL, and under both objectives student rollouts beat teacher rollouts. | CN |
| ★[2609.31900](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31900) | Understanding the Synergy between SFT, RLVR, and OPD in LLM Post-Training | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically studies how post-training stages affect each other: across nine teacher-student pairs with 2×–53× parameter ratios, shows that OPD's effect depends on teacher-student compatibility rather than teacher size. Result: RLVR adaptation of the teacher plus SFT warm-up of the student raises OPD's average accuracy at the same step count from 29.2% to 43.8%. | C |
| [2609.32573](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32573) | Trapped by Their Own Rollouts: Understanding Aggregation--Rollout Feedback in Federated On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Characterizes the feedback coupling between aggregation lag and student self-sampled data in federated OPD, along with its learning-rate sensitivity, and adapts the FedAvg step size by constraining prediction changes with teacher feedback. Result: On six math reasoning benchmarks, macro Avg@8 is 4.56–14.57 percentage points above FedAvg. | - |
| ★[2609.32722](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32722) | Scaling Properties of Same-Family On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically studies the scaling of same-family OPD in three configurations, weak-to-strong, same-base and strong-to-weak, and fits power laws of peak gold score and slope against teacher and student parameter counts. Result: Early accuracy rises roughly linearly with the square root of reverse KL; in weak-to-strong the student's peak exceeds the teacher; at equal scores, smaller teachers transfer better. | C |
| ★[2609.33136](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33136) | Leaky Students: Membership Inference against On-Policy Distillation | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | The first study of membership inference against OPD: samples new trajectories from the target model, passes the difference between their token log-probabilities and the reference-model maximum through a Leaky ReLU, and detects leakage of the teacher's privileged data. Result: Average AUROC of 0.875 over fifteen targets, versus 0.614 for the strongest baseline (0.826 with the same sampled trajectories). | C |
| ★[2609.33791](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33791) | Do We Really Need KL Divergence for On-Policy Distillation of Large Language Models? [code](https://github.com/LeapLabTHU/KL-Free-OPD) | General LLM | Multi-teacher | Argues that OPD does not need KL: it is enough for the few tokens with large teacher-student divergence to be updated toward the teacher; on this basis proposes C-MOPD, in which every sample is supervised by all teachers. Result: Giving +1 where the teacher probability is high and -1 where it is low is enough to reproduce the training pattern of reverse-KL OPD; C-MOPD outperforms MOPD. | C |
| [2609.34225](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34225) | USA: Update-aware SAM for Cross-domain On-Policy Disitllation of Language Agents | Agents | External white-box | Model merging for cross-domain OPD fails because of "cross-domain update coupling"; USA turns per-parameter update magnitudes from the warm-up phase into per-coordinate perturbation radii, lowering the curvature of the coordinates that carry the merge displacement. Result: Best in all six transfer directions across math/science/code at two student scales, more than four points above the single-domain reference on average. | N |
| [2609.34849](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34849) | When Sparse Reward Meets Dense Distillation: Training Dynamics of On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Uses an NTK cross-signal kernel to analyze the instability of mixed training with sparse RLVR rewards and dense teacher KL, identifying two failure modes: magnitude swamping and local direction conflict. Result: The gradient-norm ratio κ differs by about an order of magnitude across tasks, and once it exceeds an empirical threshold, naive mixing causes persistent training collapse. | T |
| ★[2609.35210](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35210) | Understanding On-Policy Distillation: A Mechanistic Interpretability Perspective via Sparse Crosscoders | General LLM | External white-box | Uses sparse crosscoders and swap readout to read out how the student's use of each feature changes before and after OPD, answering what OPD actually distills. Result: OPD neither creates features nor transfers teacher features, and over 98% of commonly used features change their firing rate by less than 20%; warm-up completes part of the reweighting in advance. | - |
| ★[2609.35259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35259) | On-Policy or Off-Policy Learning? A Systematic Study of Distillation Dynamics | General LLM | External white-box | In controlled strong-to-weak distillation, independently varies the rollout policy, token-level KL direction and learning rate to isolate how much on-policy sampling itself contributes. Result: Forward KL is robust to the rollout policy, and only reverse KL favors student sampling; the learning rate dominates forgetting and update sparsity. | CT |
| ★[2609.35505](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35505) | An RL View of OPD: Least Square Policy Distillation for Sample-Efficient LLM Reasoning [code](https://github.com/UNCSciML/LSPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Connects OPD's reverse KL objective to KL-regularized policy optimization, on this basis introduces value-function-style optimistic exploration and off-policy trajectory reuse, and gives a regret bound for the idealized form. Result: Avg@16 on math benchmarks is +1.59 on average; the off-policy variant matches OPD with only 25% of the rollouts. | C |
| ★[2609.37326](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37326) | Solving Without Stopping: On-Policy Distillation at Small Scale | General LLM | External white-box | Analyzes what small-model OPD actually transfers: solving ability transfers, but in thinking mode "when to stop" does not, and gives a diagnostic separating answer marking, correctness, and stopping. Result: A student's single attempt never exceeds what it could already reach in many attempts before training, and the smaller the student the further from the teacher; in thinking mode students lose the ability to end reasoning early in training. | - |
| ★[2609.37377](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37377) | Beyond Prompt Count: How Data Shapes Transfer in On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/wyy-1112/dissecting-opd) | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically studies how prompt quantity, source, and selection shape OPD transfer, and analyzes parameter and functional changes under different prompt supports. Result: 4 DAPO prompts match the math score of 3840 DeepMath prompts; changing the teacher can reverse the relative effect. | - |
| ★[2609.38666](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38666) | Understanding Off- vs On-Policy Distillation: A Tale of Distinct Training Objectives | General LLM | Multi-teacher | Analyzes sequential multi-teacher distillation: forward KL corresponds to a weighted arithmetic mixture and reverse KL to a normalized weighted geometric aggregate, giving algorithms and logarithmic regret bounds under off-policy and on-policy feedback. Result: Reverse KL better retains a confident expert's preferences but is more sensitive to teachers that assign very low probability to correct answers, and over long horizons may favor incorrect prefixes. | CN |
| [2609.38995](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38995) | When Clipping Reverses Correction: Failure Dynamics of Pointwise Forward-KL On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Directly tests the pointwise forward-KL clipping widely used in OPSD: proves the clipped objective may push the student away from rather than toward the teacher, and verifies this failure on repetition. Result: In controlled runs differing only in clipping, clipped training produces markedly more repetitions that persist to the end, with a stronger tendency to keep repeating inside them. | C |
| ★[2609.39118](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39118) | Diagnosing On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning Language Models | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Runs controlled experiments and token-level analysis on 0.6B-8B models, pointing out that the teacher signal is shaped by reasoning-mode alignment and the complete teacher prefix rather than by privileged semantics themselves. Result: OPSD helps only in narrow compatibility regimes, otherwise producing ineffective length growth, stable degradation, or behavioral collapse; the teacher signal is unstable and does not predict downstream performance. | C |
| ★[2609.39405](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39405) | No Task Vector Is an Island: A Comprehensive Study on the Composability of Task Vectors from On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Systematically studies whether OPD task vectors can complement RL teacher updates and merge across tasks, with parameter-space non-collinearity and feed-forward channel overlap analyses. Result: Across five domains and two architectures, merging OPD and RL task vectors can beat both, and cross-task merging beats RL merging in seven of eight backbone-merge-rule comparisons. | C |
| ★[2609.39494](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39494) | Disentangling Self-Distillation: Measuring and Modeling Acquisition and Retention | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Decomposes privileged-context self-distillation into three axes (rollout source, teacher coupling, KL direction) and trains every combination, then uses a controlled model to explain the acquisition-retention trade-off. Result: Across 1200 adaptation runs: teacher rollouts are clearly better only on tasks that contradict pretraining, and acquisition falls once the coupling rate is too high. | C |
| [2610.02179](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02179) | From Gradients to Capabilities: Understanding Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Multi-teacher | Dissects multi-teacher on-policy distillation via gradients, optimizer updates, and learning curves: how loss averaging, Adam's first moment, and BF16 rounding shape the teacher signal. Result: Cross-teacher gradient cosine 0.83 and cross-averaging-rule 0.96; only 7-11% of BF16 weights deviate from initialization. | CD |
| ★[2610.03185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03185) | Gains and Collapse in On-Policy Distillation:A Reinforcement Learning Perspective [code](https://github.com/HancCui/opd_hacking) | General LLM | External white-box | Explains OPD's gains and collapse from an RL perspective: the teacher acts as an implicit reward model that makes correct answers easier to sample when it is reliable, and amplifies verbose repetition when it is misaligned. Result: OPD improves performance but does not expand the student's capability; masking unhealthy responses or SFT initialization mitigates collapse. | CN |
| ★[2610.03529](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03529) | Divergence controls entropy in distillation | General LLM | - | Analyzes distillation objectives from an entropy perspective: proves that forward KL raises student entropy above the teacher's while reverse KL lowers it, so the divergence acts as an implicit entropy regularizer. Result: The low entropy of on-policy distillation comes from token-level reverse KL, not from on-policy sampling itself. | CN |
| ★[2610.04272](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04272) | Rethinking Self-Distillation for Multi-Teacher Capability Merging | General LLM | Multi-teacher | A controlled comparison for multi-teacher capability merging: compares SFT, soft labels, teacher-prefix mixing and MOPD in a same-base self-distillation setting, and re-examines published on-policy gains. Result: The four methods reach nearly identical accuracy, yet MOPD takes 14.8-23.1× the training GPU hours of SFT. | CD |
| [2610.05200](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05200) | Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Models reasoning as search on a directed acyclic graph to analyze, in one framework, the update dynamics of OPSD and SFT in continual learning and the role of pretraining diversity. Result: OPSD with hints produces sparse updates and so does not forget; SFT's dense updates overwrite old knowledge and cause catastrophic forgetting. | - |
| [2610.07654](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07654) | Does On-Policy Distillation for Safety Pose Backdoor Risks? | Safety and privacy | External white-box | Shows that a safety-aligned but backdoored teacher can pass hidden malicious behavior to a clean student through OPD, analyzes training choices that amplify the risk, and tests a mitigation that clips the KL reward. Result: At a 3% poisoning rate, student ASR reaches up to 70%; 10 poisoned samples trained for 16 epochs reach 67%; top-k KL passes on the backdoor faster than sampled-token KL. | N |
| [2610.07842](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07842) | Privileged Context as Drift in On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Systematically varies OPSD's privileged context along two axes, content (demonstration/feedback/rewrite) and source (external, or self-generated with or without a verifier), nine combinations in total, and measures target-task performance, old-task retention, reverse KL and parameter-update geometry. Result: Content matters more than source: per-token KL spans differ by 5.1×, and the update cosine is 0.571 for shared content vs 0.255 for shared source. | - |
| ★[2610.09639](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09639) | On-Policy Distillation Teaches New Skills but Not New Knowledge | General LLM | External white-box | Uses a controlled synthetic framework to separate factual knowledge from multi-step compositional skills, independently controls the extra facts or skills the teacher has, and disentangles the respective roles of reverse/forward KL and student rollouts in OPD. Result: Reverse KL OPD reliably transfers compositional skills across unseen reasoning structures but transfers almost no facts; switching to forward KL restores fact transfer. | NK |
| ★[2610.11247](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11247) | Why On-Policy Distillation Sometimes Fails: Vanishing Learning Signals [code](https://github.com/leizhao7/opd-learning-signals) | General LLM | External white-box | Analyzes early loss stagnation in OPD: treats OPD as a continuous-time dynamical system in the small-learning-rate limit, diagnoses premature decay of the gradient learning signal, and proves a local recovery guarantee when the teacher is close to the initial student. Result: After 200 steps, OPD loss falls only 25.1% with a large teacher versus 96.2% with a self-RL teacher; CKA > 0.98. | N |
| ★[2610.11291](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11291) | When Do We Need On-Policy Distillation? Distilling on Offline Student Rollouts Is Often Better | General LLM | External white-box | Questions the need for on-policy sampling: Semi-OPD, which samples offline from the initial student, often beats OPD, and the output token overlap rate characterizes when on-policy is truly needed. Result: Semi-OPD is better in 14 of 17 teacher-student pairs spanning 1.5B–235B, with up to +13.6% accuracy and 11.4× faster training. | N |
| [2610.11423](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11423) | Policy Alignment: New Signals for Membership Auditing in On-Policy Distillation | Safety and privacy | External white-box | Prompt-level membership auditing for OPD: uses Teacher Alignment Gain to judge whether the student's update moves in the direction that lowers the teacher loss on that prompt. Result: Across six datasets and three teacher-student family pairs, AUC on the MATH benchmark is 0.791–0.941, 14.6–20.6% above SOTA. | NK |
| [2610.12855](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12855) | Mitigating Capability Degradation in LLM Self-Improvement via Representation Geometry Regularization [code](https://github.com/supermachinelearner/RGR) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Finds that in OPSD training, overall metrics rise while problems answered correctly early on degrade, along with lower intra-cluster compactness and weaker inter-cluster separation in hidden representations; proposes representation geometry regularization to mitigate this. Result: Across multiple OPSD methods and benchmarks, RGR generally mitigates capability degradation while keeping the gains. | - |
| ★[2610.13205](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13205) | Rethinking Relative Policy Differences Transfer of On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/KnowledgeXLab/A2-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Defines an implicit reward as the log-probability ratio between a proxy expert and a proxy reference, giving a unified account of OPD transfer of relative policy differences, supporting difference transfer between arbitrary models and analyzing when it works. Result: A stronger expert is not necessarily better: differences induced by task training (RL/SFT) transfer reliably, while pure scale differences barely transfer; raw policy distance does not predict transferability. | - |
| ★[2610.13744](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13744) | A Privileged Teacher That Only Scores Cannot Search: Critique-Conditioned On-Policy Self-Distillation for Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Tests OPSD's full-distribution KL and token log-ratio on gold-labeled error spans and finds that neither locates errors nor pushes down erroneous tokens; instead, the teacher first writes a critique and then scores based on it. Result: With critique conditioning, the signal locates errors and corrects within error spans, raising the probability of backtracking tokens such as "Wait/But/Actually". | - |
| ★[2610.18011](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18011) | Correcting or Continuing? What On-Policy Distillation Learns from Student Errors | General LLM | External white-box | Evaluates five OPD mechanisms with Qwen-3 and Phi-4 under a unified competition-math protocol, asking specifically whether training only on problems the student currently gets wrong brings gains. Result: On the hard subset, all eight configurations decline (two-sided sign test p=0.0078) even though training KL still falls; when not focused on failed problems, the same pipeline gains 4.6 percentage points. | - |
| ★[2610.18613](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18613) | Does self-distillation improve reasoning? [code](https://github.com/sleepypandu/does-sd-improve-reasoning) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Re-examines SDPO and OPSD across code, science, tools and math, testing whether the gains of privileged-information self-distillation generalize and whether its gradient direction is sound. Result: The gains are mostly explained by overfitting or the transfer of existing reasoning behavior, and self-distillation gradients align weakly with policy gradients. | - |
| ★[2610.19145](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19145) | Demystifying On-Policy Distillation: Where Does the Teacher's Advantage Live? | General LLM | External white-box | Uses controlled token flips at teacher-student disagreement points to measure the accuracy gain from teacher corrections, and uses fork analysis to assess which teacher preferences the student actually learns. Result: Repeated flips raise accuracy 28.4%→74.5%, close to the teacher's 76.9%; mass/KL selection is 15-18 points above random. | - |
| ★[2610.20989](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20989) | Do As I Do, Not As I Say: On-Policy Distillation Inherits the Teacher in Vision-Language Models [code](https://github.com/ffy208/opd-inherits-teacher-vlm) | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Compares OPD with filtered SFT in a controlled setting on two kinds of VLM tasks with different teacher reliability, examining how teacher supervision shapes student accuracy and answer-completion behavior. Result: On ChartQA, OPD with 10% of the data is only 1.4 points behind full-data SFT; on Geometry3K it trails by 7.9 points and pushes the completion rate down from 76% to 62%. | - |
| ★[2610.25034](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25034) | Beyond the Teacher: Understanding and Preserving Distillation Overshoot in Agentic On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/Jike338/SPOD) | Agents | External white-box | Finds distillation overshoot in agentic OPD, where the student first surpasses the teacher and then falls back, attributes it to two forces, repair and erosion, and therefore masks teacher supervision that conflicts with successful choices and adds a probability floor. Result: Final success rate improves by up to 15.2 points and exceeds the teacher by up to 20.0 points. | - |
| [2610.25651](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25651) | OPDArena: Understanding Learning and Forgetting in On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/StellarCurtain/OPDArena) | General LLM | External white-box | Compares SFT, RLVR and OPD in a unified way across five pretrained models and multiple teacher sources on target-task learning, retention of general capabilities and changes in the predicted distribution. Result: Students of RLVR teachers retain old capabilities better, and the positive correlation between out-of-domain distribution change and forgetting is more stable. | - |
| [2610.25842](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25842) | Not Every Rollout Needs Distillation: Correctness-Filtered On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Gradient measurements show that the coherent-gradient share of wrong answers is about three times that of correct answers, so only some wrong rollouts are distilled and the remaining rows are dropped before the two forward passes. Result: Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B match full OPSD, with about 68% fewer executed rows and about 40% less training time. | - |
| ★[2610.26525](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26525) | A Fisher-Geometric View of Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Multi-teacher | Proves that within the same basin the multi-teacher OPD objective is, to second order, the Fisher merging objective, and from this derives a two-stage scheme that merges first and then trains branches gated by the residual spectrum. Result: On two open-source merging suites, the first-stage merge alone matches or exceeds full multi-teacher distillation. | D |
| ★[2610.30844](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30844) | Reverse-KL Policy Distillation: Statistical Guarantees and Limits | General LLM | External white-box | Analyzes the sample complexity of reverse KL distillation: online, observing only the probability of the sampled action can require far more data, offline the two feedback types are of the same order, and a truncation algorithm attaining the optimal rate is given. Result: A lower bound shows that common online objectives fail when candidates are evaluated using only past student trajectories. | - |
| [2610.30884](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30884) | Tracing Subliminal Learning Through Pretraining | Safety and privacy | External white-box | Compares subliminal learning under hard-label distillation and under on-policy soft-label distillation, tracing how preference signals transfer across initializations, corpora, scales and architectures. Result: Soft-label distillation transfers more consistently and needs less training; teacher capability and student capability develop separately, and cross-model transfer can be severely asymmetric. | - |
| [2610.31616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31616) | OP-Print: Tracing Membership Imprints in On-Policy Self-Distillation | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | The first study of membership inference against on-policy self-distillation, finding that supervision traces are sparse and concentrated at uncertain correction sites, and designing gray-box and black-box attacks accordingly. Result: All baseline attacks are near random guessing even with gray-box access, while OP-Print is effective at both access levels. | - |
| ★[2610.31918](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31918) | Beyond On-Policy vs. Off-Policy: At What Level Should LLM Agent Distillation Follow the Student? | Agents | Same model, privileged | Decomposes on-policy into two levels, the interaction history and the current action prefix, and studies their pairing, mixing ratio and training schedule in controlled experiments. Result: Student history paired with teacher action prefixes gives the strongest signal and following the student at both levels is worst; teacher context first and then fully on-policy beats a static mixture. | - |
| ★[2610.33740](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33740) | A Distribution-Matching Perspective on LLM Training Algorithms | General LLM | External white-box | Characterizes the target distributions of training algorithms at each stage in a unified distribution-matching view, uses it to argue where OPD is better or worse than classic KD, and proposes a conditional KL regularizer. Result: OPD wins when the model is close to the target distribution and loses when it is far; when the target distribution depends on the model, accumulated variance makes it degenerate toward determinism. | - |
| [2610.38383](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38383) | How Much Do Teacher Bridges Matter in Interleaved On-Policy Distillation? | General LLM | External white-box | Compares three loss treatments of teacher bridge tokens on the same batch of trajectories and separates gradients by who wrote the text; a pre-registered test finds no accuracy gain from teacher text used as context or as a training target. Result: A bridge accounts for only 2.7% of tokens, its gradient is nearly orthogonal to the student token gradient, and the three losses are equivalent within ±5 points. | - |
| ★[2610.39020](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39020) | Distilling the Shape of Uncertainty: What On-Policy Distillation Learns and How to Predict It [code](https://github.com/maybenotime/opd-esc) | General LLM | External white-box | Uses entropy shape (the per-token entropy sequence) to characterize the where-to-branch behavior that OPD learns, proposes ESC to measure teacher-student agreement, shows its causal role by intervention, and uses it to select teachers in advance. Result: ESC measured before distillation picks the best teacher for both students, outperforming existing predictive metrics. | - |

## Objectives and signal design (113)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [2608.00782](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00782) | Distill Where You Fail: Recovering Learning Signals of Negative RL-Groups from Adaptive Teacher Guidance | General LLM | External white-box | Shows that naively adding GRPO and OPD degrades performance; RSTG distills only on negative zero-variance groups, selects tokens by student entropy and teacher-student divergence, and adds SFT on the teacher's correct trajectories. Result: Over naive GRPO+OPD, +4.02% on math and +3.05% on code. | CTN |
| [2608.01263](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01263) | Distill What the Student Can See: Fisher-Projected On-Policy Distillation for Vision-Language Models | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Since a compact VLM cannot realize the complete teacher distribution, FP-OPD uses visual perturbations to estimate the student's local visual tangent space and projects the teacher-student log-probability gap onto it as the target. Result: In 8B→2B distillation, all seven multimodal benchmarks improve, with an average of +1.60 points over standard OPD. | CTNJ |
| [2608.02942](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02942) | OPTD: On-Policy Transition Distillation with Consistency-Guided Adaptive Compression for Few-Step Diffusion Language Models | Diffusion language models | Own earlier checkpoint | Few-step dLLM distillation: samples intermediate states from the student's own trajectories, has a frozen teacher pick outcome-aligned future candidates, and merges the longest mergeable prefix into one step. Result: Consistently better quality-efficiency trade-off on four math and code benchmarks, with the best quality-constrained AUP. | CT |
| [2608.03632](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03632) | When Teachers Mislead: Spurious-Signal-Aware On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/jjjyinuo/SA-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Points out that a teacher's token-level judgments may come from input-agnostic spurious signals such as language priors and formatting conventions, and filters out tokens with low input-groundedness and high divergence before distilling. Result: Better than naive OPD and existing selective distillation methods in both LLM and VLM settings (no numbers given in the abstract). | CT |
| [2608.03929](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03929) | Latent Reward Registers for Diffusion Preference Alignment [code](https://github.com/Guanys-dar/latent-reward-register) | Image, video and audio generation | - | Attaches learnable register tokens to a frozen DiT, estimates terminal preference directly from intermediate latents to obtain a dense reward field, and converts it into per-step distillation targets. Result: RG-OPD outperforms online RL baselines and cuts GPU hours by up to 33×. | J |
| [2608.04419](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04419) | SPOT: Sparse Probing and Outcome Calibration for On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/QuZikun/SPOT) | General LLM | External white-box | Allocates a limited probing budget with a position-level score (teacher entropy, top-k probability mass, student-teacher mismatch), then calibrates the distillation target with verifier-scored student continuations. Result: Improves reasoning across several student models and reasoning benchmarks while balancing solution quality and coverage (no numbers given). | CTN |
| [2608.04887](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04887) | STEP-OPD: Rethinking Output Targets and Internal Dynamics in On-Policy Distillation for Diffusion Models | Image, video and audio generation | Multi-teacher | Adds the velocity difference between the task teacher and the shared base model on top of the teacher velocity as an extrapolation, and aligns the direction and magnitude of blockwise representation changes between student and teacher. Result: GenEval rises from 0.927 to 0.961, and the unified student surpasses each single-task teacher. | CTJ |
| [2608.05802](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05802) | On-Policy Delta Distillation for Multilingual Math Reasoning | General LLM | External white-box | Uses the probability gap between a post-trained teacher and its base model as the learning signal, doing OPD for multilingual math reasoning in English, Korean and Japanese. Result: Gains are especially clear for Korean and Japanese; English-only OPD pushes the response language toward English. | CT |
| [2608.06213](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06213) | Gryphon-v2: One Model in Place of a Cascade - Generate-and-Rank Recommender with Rollout Distillation | Other applications | External white-box | A unified generate-and-rank recommendation model in which a training-only teacher ranker supplies the only ranking supervision, on current-decoder rollouts and logged impressions. Result: In an online A/B test it replaces a cascade of 15+ candidate generators and increases active users by 1.41%. | C |
| [2608.06243](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06243) | DASH: Divergence-Adaptive Supervision Horizons for On-Policy Self-Distillation of Reasoning Models [code](https://github.com/DBtxy/DASH-OPSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Maps the gap between the local divergence and the sequence mean to an adaptive propagation gate, then adjusts token-level supervision weights by backward multi-step aggregation. Result: Beats vanilla OPSD under matched configurations across three math benchmarks and three model scales, with no extra forward cost. | CT |
| [2608.07935](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07935) | Adaptive Supervised Anchoring for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Shows that rollout drift weakens teacher supervision, and uses token-level alignment to adaptively mix rollout distillation with cross-entropy on canonical contexts. Result: Better than OPSD across model scales and on two task families, while preserving general capabilities. | CTN |
| [2608.08176](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08176) | Matching Supervision to the Student's Learning Capacity: A Unified Framework for On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/lauvlalala/USD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Unifies token selection and privileged-information strength into one optimization problem with a learning-difficulty budget, deciding both online with a single dual variable. Result: Better than OPSD and than token-side and PI-side baselines across model scales and reasoning benchmarks. | CTN |
| [2608.09233](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09233) | DreOPD: Degraded-Reference Extrapolative On-Policy Distillation for Flow-matching Models [code](https://sleepy1231.github.io/DreOPD/) | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Flow-matching image generation: turns implicit reward extrapolation into closed-form velocity regression, trains by extrapolating on the student's own sampled trajectories, and widens the contrast with a lightly degraded reference. Result: Better than OPD and multi-task RL under both single-teacher and multi-teacher settings, surpassing the expert teachers on most metrics. | CTJ |
| [2608.09447](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09447) | WDL-OPD: Weak-Driven On-Policy Distillation via Mixture-Constrained Co-Training | General LLM | External white-box | Two trainable policies work together: an anchor policy samples and an auxiliary policy evaluates the same state, and the geometric mixture of their token distributions is aligned to a frozen teacher with reverse KL. Result: Strongest in all four scale-domain settings; MATH500 rises from 0.630 to 0.685 at 4B. | CTN |
| [2608.09745](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09745) | SR-OPSD: Self-Referenced On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Anchors on a normalized geometric target formed from the self-teacher and the frozen initial policy, then minimizes the forward Rényi divergence to the student, where the order controls the power weighting of the probability ratio. Result: Strong results across model families and scales on scientific reasoning, tool use, math and code. | CTNHK |
| ★[2608.09836](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09836) | Mismatch Matters: On-Policy Distillation Beyond Token Agreement [code](https://github.com/yzc-666/TIDE) | General LLM | External white-box | Reveals degenerate agreement, where the student games high token agreement with repetition loops; the fix is to handle mismatch instead, with a bounded Hellinger term suppressing excess mass and teacher top-K injection filling in what is missing. Result: Under strong mismatch, Avg@8 rises from 6.9% to 20.3% and response length shrinks 3.6×. | CTN |
| [2608.11698](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11698) | REOPD: Reliability-Adaptive Reward Extrapolation for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | A global coefficient for reward extrapolation easily leads to reward hacking: a token-level compatibility weight times a batch-level adaptive budget is used instead, extrapolating only along reliable teacher-reference directions. Result: Better than G-OPD in two multi-teacher domains and in single-teacher math, on par on code, and needs no verifier or extra rollouts. | CT |
| [2608.13387](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13387) | CROP: Task Relevance via Counterfactuals for Selective On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Builds original-paraphrase-counterfactual triples and, with the student rollout fixed, measures the task relevance of each position by paraphrase-calibrated counterfactual sensitivity, then allocates OPD supervision selectively on that basis. Result: Beats the strongest non-CROP selector by 1.92 and 2.96 points in two teacher-student settings, respectively. | CT |
| [2608.14685](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14685) | Rethinking Reverse KL as Adaptive Entropy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Splits on-policy reverse KL into a teacher-fitting term and a student-entropy term, proves that the token-level optimal student is a temperature variant of the teacher distribution, and dynamically calibrates imitation strength with teacher entropy. Result: Better overall on instruction following and math reasoning, with generally improved alignment of teacher and student distributions and entropy. | CT |
| [2608.14728](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14728) | Tail-Aware Top-k On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/HuipengHuang/TA-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Adds, outside the teacher's top-k, one token that carries the tail probability before computing reverse KL, preventing top-k normalization from steadily raising the student's tail probability and entropy. Result: Improves Avg@8 by up to 8.05 points on common benchmarks. | CT |
| [2608.16316](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16316) | Deep Thought Alignment: Trajectory-Level Latent Distillation for Video Reasoning | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Adds trajectory-level hidden-state distillation on top of output-layer OPD: it aligns the hidden state at the final position of each student trajectory and has the student's middle and later layers progressively align with ever deeper teacher layers. Result: Consistently beats output-layer-only OPD on six video reasoning benchmarks, with the largest gains in few-frame and long-video settings. | TJ |
| ★[2608.16333](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16333) | Step-Level On-Policy Distillation: Interpolating Between On-Policy Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning | General LLM | External white-box | Has the teacher continue from intermediate states of student trajectories to write a complete repair path as step-level supervision; at the two extremes of step length it reduces to SFT and to approximate OPD, respectively. Result: Average success rate on ALFWorld is 13.4 points above vanilla OPD, and it beats SFT and OPD on both reasoning and agentic tasks. | CT |
| [2608.19181](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19181) | Beyond Teacher Likelihood: Group-Calibrated On-Policy Distillation for Long-Context Reasoning [code](https://github.com/SolereZhang/GC-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Normalizes verifier rewards and trajectory-level OPD scores separately within each group, uses their signed residual to capture teacher-verifier disagreement, then spreads the residual over tokens according to relative OPD advantage. Result: Five-benchmark average: Qwen3-4B 29.08→40.47 and 8B 35.12→44.65, both above the original OPD. | CTN |
| [2608.19408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19408) | Beyond Imitation: Filtering On-Policy Distillation by Reasoning Progress | General LLM | External white-box | Within one trajectory, ranks reasoning segments separately by teacher reward and by independently estimated reasoning progress, suppressing the distillation reward where the two rankings conflict while keeping useful teacher guidance. Result: Consistent gains over standard OPD, especially in reasoning performance. | CTN |
| [2608.20831](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20831) | STAR-OPD: Structured Aspect-Cascade-Aware On-Policy Reward Distillation for ABSA Quadruple Extraction | Other applications | External white-box | Instantiates general OPD for ABSA quadruple extraction: a cascade-aware set-structured reward on student rollouts corrects broken target-aspect bindings and target hallucination. Result: Beats off-policy and general on-policy distillation baselines on E-ABSA20K and SemEval-2014, and reduces target hallucination. | C |
| [2608.22364](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22364) | WAM-OPD: Joint Video-Action Supervision for World Action Model Post-Training with On-Policy Distillation | Embodied | External white-box | On histories produced by the student's own execution, asks a stronger teacher for paired future-video and action targets; the student fits both supervision streams at once and still keeps one-step video and action generation at deployment. Result: Mean over the 12 RoboTwin 2.0 tasks 33.8%→65.7%; four real-robot tasks 51.4%→64.6%. | CTJ |
| [2608.26550](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26550) | SPEAR: Distilling Domain-Adaptive Reasoning Skeletons via Sequential Symbolic Alignment in Reinforcement Learning [code](https://github.com/zhuochunli/SPEAR) | General LLM | External white-box | Projects reasoning trajectories into domain-adaptive symbolic milestones and aligns student exploration with teacher milestones via the longest common subsequence, yielding a training-free dense process reward for sequence-level on-policy distillation. | C |
| [2608.26583](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26583) | SOLO: Stable Omni-terrain Long-Horizon Perceptive Humanoid Locomotion [code](https://sunpihai-up.github.io/solo/) | Embodied | External white-box | Perceptive humanoid locomotion control: adds the next-state teacher-student divergence to the PPO reward so that GAE propagates future divergence penalties back to earlier actions, supplying the temporal credit assignment that pointwise imitation lacks. Result: Average pass rate of 97.5% on stress-test terrains and 96% on stepping stones, and completes a 1.5 km outdoor route zero-shot. | - |
| [2608.26872](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26872) | Self-OPD: On-Policy Distillation for Flow Matching Models without Teacher | Image, video and audio generation | - | Teacher-free OPD for flow matching models: at each step it forks the deterministic prediction into K SDE candidates and continues sampling with ODE, then computes advantages from rewards relative to a self-reference baseline for pull-push updates. Result: Beats prior RL and OPD methods on single-reward and mixed-reward benchmarks without needing a task-specific teacher. | CJ |
| [2608.27960](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27960) | When Teacher Guidance Misleads: Reward-Aligned On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Teacher guidance on student prefixes can diverge from the outcome reward; RA-OPD checks whether each trajectory's distillation return agrees with its outcome reward and filters out inconsistent trajectories, adding no extra compute. Result: Significantly beats standard OPD across the Qwen3 and DeepSeek-R1 series and seven math and three code benchmarks. | CTN |
| [2609.01947](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01947) | On-Policy Distillation Meets Off-Policy GRPO: Training Compact Instruction-Following Rerankers | Other applications | External white-box | Two-stage training of compact rerankers: first strengthens a 4B teacher with off-policy GRPO on LLM-judge feedback, then has a 1B student sample rankings from its own policy and receive soft rewards derived from the teacher. Result: nDCG@6 on MAIR-11 reaches 0.7670, 4.6 points above offline listwise KD. | C |
| [2609.02817](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02817) | Cliff: Learning Process Rewards from the First Mistake | General LLM | Black-box | Uses an off-the-shelf LLM teacher to locate the first error in each rollout, splits the trajectory into a correct prefix and an erroneous suffix, and converts them into token-level positive and negative advantages as RLVR reward shaping. Result: Consistent gains across 12 scenarios, 15% above on-policy distillation and 7% above GRPO, and it works even with a teacher of average ability. | - |
| [2609.06100](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06100) | VERPO: Verified Evidence Regularized Policy Optimization | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Splits evidence-conditioned self-distillation guidance into an evidence-free reference term and an evidence-induced sign correction, turned into token-level credit through selective acceptance and Fisher cost scaling. Result: Avoids optimization collapse on five scientific reasoning and tool-use tasks, with the best multi-task average and especially clear gains for small models. | T |
| [2609.08337](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08337) | Distillation as Probability Transport: Routed On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Recasts OPD as teacher-guided probability transport, decomposing teacher-student disagreement into student excess sources and teacher deficit sinks, pairing them explicitly and optimizing paired log-odds. Result: Consistently better than OPD with sampled reverse KL across four teacher-student settings and four math reasoning benchmarks, with higher routing fidelity and lower background leakage. | CTN |
| [2609.08341](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08341) | TV-Regulated OPD: Direction Matters in On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Analyzes the training instability caused by high variance in OPD supervision signals, finds that keeping only the sign of the token-level advantage matches standard OPD, and uses total variation to shape bounded smooth advantages. Result: TV-OPD performs better late in training and with lower variance across a range of settings. | CNK |
| [2609.08798](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08798) | Eliciting Weak-to-Strong Generalization with On-Policy Reverse Distillation [code](https://github.com/raymin0223/on_policy_reverse_distillation) | General LLM | Weak teacher | A weak teacher is not used as a fitting target: the teacher's shift direction relative to its reference policy is measured on student rollouts, and only the component of the student verifier gradient along that direction is amplified. Result: On math, logic, code and visual tool use, it reaches higher performance than existing RL and distillation with fewer student updates. | CTD |
| [2609.11768](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11768) | A Unified Per-Token Gating Family for On-Policy Distillation: FKL/RKL Mixing with Multi-Channel and Bias Coefficients | General LLM | External white-box | Unifies the per-token FKL/RKL gating of EOPD and ToDi into a four-coefficient parameterization that serves as a common coordinate system for comparing gating designs. Result: On TweetEval, 33 of 36 comparable cells beat the single-channel 1D restriction, but a three-seed replication with n=3 is not significant. | C |
| [2609.16459](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16459) | OPD-Aha: From Linguistic Momentum to Visual Reflection in Multimodal On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/Echochef/OPD-Aha) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Finds that once the student misreads an image early on, teacher and student agree on the same hallucination and supervision stops working, so the distillation target is rebuilt from the difference between the same teacher's predictions on the real image and on a visually empty input, suppressing continuations that contradict the image. Result: The student spontaneously uses reflection tokens such as wait and actually to interrupt wrong reasoning and turn back to visual evidence, with broad gains on fine-grained perception and complex multimodal reasoning benchmarks. | CTNJ |
| [2609.16937](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16937) | Beyond Token-Local Imitation: Reward-Compatible Temporal Credit Assignment for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | A unified temporal credit view shows that token-level OPD is a temporal approximation of the sequence-level reverse KL gradient, proposes a discounted γOPD, and brings in verifiable outcome feedback through a bounded mixture. Result: Consistently better than existing OPD methods on math and code reasoning under the standard, scale-mismatched and multi-teacher settings. | CNK |
| [2609.18321](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18321) | Trajectory Learnability for Offline On-Policy Distillation with Imperfect Teachers | General LLM | External white-box | Offline OPD bakes in imperfect teacher supervision: training first runs on problems the teacher solves, then the signed change in each token's likelihood on trajectories for problems the teacher fails serves as a learnability weight. Result: Up to 2.7 percentage points over the offline OPD baseline on math and code, matching or beating online OPD on several benchmarks with only 2 GPUs and about 22 GPU hours. | CN |
| [2609.21432](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21432) | GVPO++: Group Variance Policy Optimization for LLM Post-Training and On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Embeds the analytical solution of KL-constrained reward maximization into gradient weighting, so the gradient equals the mean squared error between the centered implicit reward and the centered true reward, and extends the framework naturally to OPD and a family of extended OPD objectives. Result: Guarantees consistency with the unique optimum of the KL-constrained reward maximization objective and allows flexible sampling distributions without importance sampling. | CN |
| [2609.21619](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21619) | Calibrating Teacher--Student Discrepancy for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Teacher-student discrepancy mixes in the teacher's own shift, which privileged information amplifies; positive and negative privileged interventions estimate the region of that self-shift, and only the discrepancy component beyond it is kept as the optimization signal. Result: Only about 52-65% of the original teacher-student discrepancy is kept as signal, and math reasoning stays better than standard OPD and its variants across model scales. | CTN |
| ★[2609.24432](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24432) | 1% of Tokens Can Be Enough: On Gradient Estimation in On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/BruceSheng1202/IER-OPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Studies the noise of estimating gradients from a single sampled token in sparse OPD: an information efficiency ratio IER in signal-to-noise form is defined under information geometry and combined with a usefulness score to select tokens. Result: On math and medical reasoning, IER improves existing selectors, and a token budget of 0.1%-1% already matches or beats full OPD. | CNX |
| [2609.27421](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27421) | Counterfactual Constraint-Conditioned On-Policy Distillation for Multi-Constraint Instruction Following | General LLM | External white-box | OPD for multi-constraint instruction following: instead of giving the teacher extra information, each constraint in the teacher's conditioning is ablated in turn, and the leave-one-out token-level log-likelihood difference is added to the OPD reward as a shaping term. Result: Best average across two Qwen teacher-student pairs and seven benchmarks, and the 1.5B student beats its 7B RL teacher on MulDimIF. | CN |
| [2609.27869](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27869) | Learning What to Activate: Combinatorial Capability Allocation for Long-Horizon Multimodal Agents | Agents | External white-box | Selecting capability subsets for long-horizon multimodal agents: a strong teacher compares the marginal net value of candidate capabilities at states the student visits, which is turned into an autoregressive subset policy, with bilevel OPD. Result: Better than the SOTA baselines compared against in long-horizon multimodal environments and under controlled shifts in capability demand, with no teacher queries at inference. | - |
| [2609.28778](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28778) | Reward-Tilted On-Policy Distillation for Acoustic Grounding in Audio-Language Models [code](https://github.com/KaiyangLi1992/RT-OPD) | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Builds a reward from the teacher's log-probability difference with and without audio input, reshapes the teacher distribution and then distills on student trajectories with reverse KL to strengthen acoustic grounding. Result: The 3B model reaches 72.72% on MMAU, the highest at its scale and competitive with some 7B/8B models. | CTJ |
| [2609.28845](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28845) | LastOPD: Taming Collapse in Latent On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/Muyiiiii/LastOPD) | General LLM | External white-box | Finds that hidden-state alignment OPD rises then collapses and that better alignment gives worse performance, so only the last layer is aligned, with a 10-step cross-fade into token-level OPD. Result: On MATH-500 it is 5.55 and 4.02 points above pure token-level OPD and needs about half as many steps to reach the same score. | CN |
| [2609.29142](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29142) | Not Every Token Is Worth Distilling: Selective Supervision for Direct-OPD [code](https://github.com/Luli3220/S2D-OPD) | General LLM | Weak teacher | Constructs a counterexample showing that the log-ratio signal of Direct-OPD stays unchanged as probability mass vanishes, and instead keeps supervision only on the top 10% of states in each response by teacher-student JSD. Result: With two teachers and four students from 1.7B to 8B, seven of eight settings beat dense Direct-OPD on AIME/HMMT. | CN |
| [2609.30864](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30864) | Persistent Negatives for Adversarial Black-Box On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Black-box | In adversarial distillation for black-box OPD, uses historical teacher-student contrast samples as persistent negatives to stabilize the discriminator reward, while GRPO stays on-policy on fresh student trajectories. Result: Consistently beats existing methods across two student types, three judges and four dialogue benchmarks, with smoother discriminator trajectories. | C |
| [2609.32781](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32781) | CT-OPD: Counterfactual Trace On-Policy Distillation for Diffusion Vision-Language Models | Diffusion language models | External white-box | Takes the masks of unresolved positions at each stage of the student's reverse process, re-tokenizes the teacher's answer with the student vocabulary and rebuilds partial states, so supervised positions follow the student's trajectory while context and targets stay consistent. Result: Average gains of up to 9.80 points across nine benchmarks; on a unified understanding-and-generation architecture, understanding and generation improve together. | CJ |
| [2609.33301](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33301) | Hesitation-Aware On-Policy Distillation for Diffusion Language Models | Diffusion language models | External white-box | Trajectory-style OPD supervises only committed positions; this work extends teacher distribution matching to the "hesitation" proposals at every masked position and assigns weights using hindsight information from the completed trajectory. Result: Hesitation occupies 24% of positions yet carries 66% of the teacher-student divergence; best average score on five math and code benchmarks. | C |
| [2609.33409](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33409) | Dense Is Not Enough: Hierarchical Supervision Allocation for Long-Horizon On-Policy Distillation | Agents | External white-box | Frames long-horizon OPD as hierarchical supervision allocation: from coarse to fine, it locates the decisions to intervene on, verifies whether teacher guidance improves later behavior, and then focuses on key teacher-student conflict tokens. Result: Consistently beats naive OPD and curriculum/selection baselines across multiple long-horizon agent benchmarks and teacher-student configurations. | CX |
| [2609.33455](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33455) | What Shared Prefixes Hide: Trajectory Dropout for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Identifies prefix-induced supervision attenuation (PISA): during training, randomly drops part of the student's reasoning trajectory while the teacher still sees the full trajectory, strengthening supervision on overconfident predictions and prefix-sensitive positions. Result: Consistent gains across multiple teacher-student scales and six math benchmarks, plus gains on two out-of-domain benchmarks, with negligible overhead. | - |
| [2609.33711](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33711) | DuoOPD: Learning from Joint Teacher-Student Outcomes for Multi-Task On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/YongYuanDeAo/DuoOPD) | General LLM | External white-box | In multi-task OPD, the student's correctness sets the feedback direction and the joint teacher-student outcome sets how the teacher is used: when only the teacher is correct, its answer serves as context to score the student's failed response; when only the student is correct, the response is reinforced as a whole. Result: On Qwen3 and Llama, macro-average accuracy beats all five baselines, 2.58 and 5.98 points above OPD. | C |
| [2609.34234](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34234) | MAS-OPD: On-Policy Distillation for Multi-agent Systems | Agents | External white-box | OPD for multi-agent systems: defines a role advantage as the difference between teacher signals conditioned on the target role and on non-target roles to form a division of labor, and attributes interaction conflicts, then gives them only to the teacher as privileged information. Result: Highest average score at both student scales on code and math benchmarks, with clearer role division and collaboration. | ND |
| [2609.34386](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34386) | Look Before You Select: Rethinking Vocabulary Sparsification in On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | First uses the full-vocabulary teacher correction to judge which tokens' corrections matter, then backpropagates only through the selected token logits, with sign-residual compensation and per-position budget allocation to cut GPU memory. Result: Beats Sampled/TopK in six task-scale settings, with gradient cosine similarity reaching 99% and backward-pass memory cut by 70.5%. | CN |
| [2609.34447](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34447) | Unbiased Top-k Estimation for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | On top of top-k OPD, additionally uses student-sampled tokens to restore, in expectation, the discarded tail probability mass, giving an unbiased estimate of the reverse KL gradient. Result: Experiments show TT-OPD clearly outperforms the other OPD variants tested (the abstract gives no specific numbers). | CN |
| [2609.34581](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34581) | Counterfactual Attention Policy Distillation for Temporal Video Grounding | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Measures counterfactual influence by how the teacher's output distribution changes when each temporal group is masked, and uses it to calibrate teacher attention and weight token-level distillation, so the student learns the temporal evidence that supports boundary prediction. Result: Trained for one epoch on 2500 samples, average recall on TimeLens improves by 12.0% relative to GRPO, while general video understanding is preserved. | CJ |
| [2609.35319](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35319) | Teacher-Student Gaps Are Not Enough: Outcome-Guided On-Policy Distillation for Multi-Turn Autonomous Agents | Agents | External white-box | Points out that a large token-level teacher-student gap does not mean an intervention is warranted, a supervision-benefit mismatch; instead calibrates relative trajectory weights by the final task outcomes of paired student continuations, then weights teacher supervision. Result: Success rates on three agent benchmarks improve by 3.6~17.7 points over vanilla OPD, and by up to +7.0 points over the strongest baseline. | C |
| [2609.35430](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35430) | Can Generative Retrievers Learn Semantic IDs Without Forgetting How to Speak? | Other applications | Own earlier checkpoint | During generative retrieval fine-tuning, uses the frozen original model for forward-KL on-policy distillation regularization on student-generated prefixes to preserve natural language generation, and adaptively adjusts the preservation strength. Result: WikiText-2 forward KL drops by 81.3~93.8% on MS MARCO and by 81.2~85.2% on NQ. | - |
| [2609.35517](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35517) | Reward-Aligned Reweighting for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | External white-box | Continuously reallocates the teacher's token-level supervision weights by outcome consistency and teacher-student divergence magnitude, replacing uniform imitation and hard filtering. Result: Beats standard OPD on all seven math benchmarks, with average gains of +3.5/+2.4 points for 1.7B/4B students, and +1.6 points on code. | C |
| ★[2609.36484](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36484) | The Teacher Is a Direction, Not a Destination: Extrapolating RL-Induced Representation Residuals in On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/xixixixixxxx/RIDE) | General LLM | External white-box | Output-space extrapolation is attenuated by the anisotropy of the LM head; RIDE instead extrapolates in representation space along the teacher's per-layer residual direction relative to its pre-RL checkpoint. Result: On all four base/RL teacher pairs it approaches or exceeds the RL teacher, and it is the only method to do so on average. | CK |
| [2609.36601](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36601) | SAKI: Maximal-Coupling-Routed Teacher Supervision for On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/Miteto-sudo/SAKI) | General LLM | External white-box | Under teacher-guided rollouts, uses maximal coupling and routes supervision by accept/correction events: accepted positions get sampled-token reverse KL, correction positions supervise the teacher's highest-probability token. Result: The correction probability is exactly the TV distance; across seven math benchmarks both metrics improve for 1.7B/0.6B students. | C |
| [2609.37851](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37851) | FlowMap-OPD: Rollout--Kernel Separation for On-Policy Distillation of Few-Step Flow-Map Generators | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Decouples student-state acquisition from teacher-student distribution comparison for few-step flow-map generators, characterizes this separation via state marginals, and compares flow-map, induced-velocity, and instantaneous-velocity distribution supervision. Result: In cross-capacity ImageNet experiments, instantaneous-velocity distribution supervision is best; in text-to-image it consolidates multiple specialists well and surpasses multi-reward Flow-Map GRPO. | C |
| [2609.37925](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37925) | Rollout-Marginal Distillation for Long-Horizon Autoregressive Video Generation [code](https://cjeen.github.io/RMD) | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Autoregressive video generation keeps its own rollout history but scores each chunk independently against a chunk teacher to avoid whole-sequence DMD accommodating artifacts, then adds video-level DMD to restore coherence. Result: Maintains high visual quality on rollouts far beyond the training horizon, outperforming video-level DMD baselines. | C |
| ★[2609.38025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38025) | Dr. OPD: Learning What to Follow for Optimal On-Policy Distillation of Large Language Models | General LLM | External white-box | Treats the weights of token-level teacher supervision as bilevel-optimization variables: the inner level updates the student by weighted OPD, the outer level selects weights to maximize the student's expected reward, solved alternately with a closed-form weight update.
