Result: Qwen3-1.7B reaches 87.0% balanced tool-use accuracy, above the 85.0% of off-policy token distillation. | - |
| [2608.06065](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06065) | The Next Screenshot Knows: Gated Hindsight Distillation for Mobile GUI Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Uses the next screenshot as training-time privileged information, has a parameter-sharing teacher re-score student rollouts, and distills only when the student fails and the teacher recovers the action. Result: Both vision-language models beat GRPO on AndroidWorld and AndroidLab. | C |
| ★[2608.06296](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06296) | On-Policy Self-Distillation without Any Supervision [code](https://github.com/williamium3000/u-opsd) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Samples many rollouts and votes under a self-consistency threshold to obtain a pseudo solution as the condition, then self-distills only on the disagreeing completions, using no external supervision at all. Result: Qwen3 non-thinking mode gains +8.5%/10.7% over the base at 4B/8B and beats OPSD by 3.2%/2.3% on average. | CTHK |
| [2608.06347](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06347) | RP-OPSD: Reasoning-Pivot-Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation for Multilingual Reasoning Transfer [code](https://github.com/NJUNLP/RP-OPSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Locates reasoning-pivot tokens from the distribution shift between two teacher views, with and without the English reference solution, and concentrates privileged distillation and reference anchoring there. Result: Better than multilingual baselines and OPSD variants on math reasoning covering 17 languages. | CT |
| [2608.08764](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08764) | Learning from Consensus and Disagreement: Unsupervised On-Policy Self-Distillation with Minority-Trajectory Contrast | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Constructs privileged information without supervision: answer consensus conditions a frozen self-teacher to give dense supervision on fresh student trajectories, while minority trajectories are penalized through a KTO-style negative branch to guard against a false consensus. Result: Clearly better than self-generated baselines on competition-level math benchmarks, and stable against a false consensus. | CTH |
| [2608.08960](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08960) | Reading is not Reasoning: Bridging the Agentic Policy Gap in Vision-Text Compression | Agents | Same model, privileged | Supervises the visual-history policy with the same model's stronger text-history policy: offline trajectory self-distillation plus online dense supervision at the states it visits during RL. Result: 5.0/3.4 points above AgentOCR on SearchQA and 15.6/14.5 points above it on ALFWorld. | TNJ |
| [2608.09555](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09555) | Bidirectional Context Self-Distillation for Reinforcement Learning of Skill-Based LLM Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Takes two skill-context views of the same trajectory, augmented (with Meta-Skill guidance) and ablated, and relabels RL advantages with the complementary token-level signals. Result: Overall best across model scales on ALFWorld and WebShop, with ablations confirming that the two views are complementary. | C |
| [2608.09789](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09789) | ADOPD: Reference-Privileged On-Policy Distillation for MLLM-Based Industrial Anomaly Detection [code](https://github.com/withTai/ADOPD) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Internalizes inference-time reference-image comparison into the weights: the teacher evaluates student rollouts under matched and mismatched references, the log ratio sets the direction and the likelihood gap sets the sequence weight. Result: 77.31% average zero-shot MMAD, 6.14 points above the Qwen3-VL-4B base. | TJ |
| [2608.09826](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09826) | Distill Skills into Weights, Not Prompts: Abstract Skills as Privileged Signals for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Targets the absent gradient from zero-variance groups in RLVR: the teacher sees an abstract skill card with the answer filtered out while the student sees only the question, and annealed skewed distillation is applied on student prefixes. Result: Zero-variance groups account for 63.0-68.0%; avg@8 on five math benchmarks is 2.46-12.01 above GRPO. | CTN |
| [2608.09931](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09931) | Perception Before Supervision: Self-Contained Visual Distillation from Counterfactual Blind Spots | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Finds visual blind spots with a three-gate counterfactual criterion: zooming in sharpens the answer distribution while removing the region barely changes full-image behavior, and these become dense contrastive self-distillation supervision. Result: On Qwen3-VL-8B it beats six self-evolving baselines across twelve benchmarks with no regression. | J |
| [2608.10286](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10286) | TRACE-GS: On-Policy Trajectory Distillation with Privileged Geometric Conditioning for Sparse-View 3DGS Restoration | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | Sparse-view 3DGS restoration: the teacher is conditioned on privileged geometry from extra views and gives an alignment target at every state visited along the student's denoising rollout. Result: Claimed to be the first sparse-view 3DGS restoration method that derives on-policy supervision from privileged geometry. | C |
| [2608.10775](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10775) | SkillLens: Visual Skill Cards for Retrieval-Augmented GUI Action Prediction and On-Policy Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | Compresses interaction trajectories into visual skill cards that bind applicability cues to verification signals; CardDistill then trains the student with the card evidence as the teacher's privileged context. Result: CardDistill gives the Qwen3-VL-2B student a further 12.0 and 3.2 points. | CJ |
| [2608.11191](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11191) | Test-Time Self-Evolving GUI Visual Grounding via Reflection-Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | A post-deployment self-evolution loop: after exploring an unfamiliar interface a reflector evaluates it, a conditioned self-teacher turns the reflection into dense token supervision, and contrastive calibration is applied. Result: Average accuracy on six benchmarks is 7.4% above the base model, claimed as the first test-time adaptation for GUI grounding. | CTJ |
| [2608.11660](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11660) | Hybrid-Policy Self-Editing for Composable Unstructured Knowledge Editing [code](https://github.com/lliutianc/hpse) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Treats knowledge editing as self-distillation from the same model's privileged context; since the new knowledge is outside the coverage of the original rollout, the missing fact is inserted where coverage fails, forming a hybrid trajectory. Result: Theoretical analysis shows it is better than pure on-policy distillation, and it gives plug-and-play gains on four backbones and two editors. | C |
| [2608.11898](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11898) | On-Policy Self-Distillation for Multi-Dialect ASR: Mastering Dialects, Retaining Mandarin | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Finishes multi-dialect ASR adaptation with OPSD: the student trains on prefixes it decoded itself, while a frozen teacher gives soft targets with the reference transcription as privileged context. Result: Under the same data and schedule, dialect recognition improves without raising Mandarin CER. | TJ |
| [2608.11967](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11967) | LoongReflect: Boosting Long-Horizon Reflection in Search Agents via Global Perspective Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | Models reflection as a memory-control policy: a fast path has a privileged teacher supervise only the reflection and backtracking tokens, a slow path optimizes full trajectories with outcome-based GRPO, and the two are coordinated in a lookahead manner. Result: Consistently better than outcome-only RL and self-distillation baselines on multi-hop RAG and math reasoning benchmarks. | C |
| [2608.12764](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12764) | Beyond Outcome Rewards: Step-Level Self-Distilled Policy Optimization for Deep Search Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Uses evidence anchors extracted from web pages as privileged information, turning teacher-student disagreement into step-level advantage weights that are applied only to incorrect trajectories, leaving correct ones untouched. Result: On Qwen3-8B it consistently beats GRPO on BrowseComp/GAIA/FRAMES, with only about 5% extra cost per step. | CT |
| [2608.12957](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12957) | I-SDPO: Instance-Level Adaptive Self-Distillation Policy Optimization | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Routes per instance: when the whole group is wrong it uses privileged self-distillation, and if there is any success it keeps GRPO; a local analysis characterizes when the teacher direction agrees with the reward direction and gives a lower bound on the bias. Result: Best on all four scientific domains of SciKnowEval, with mean@16 rising from 56.67% to 70.31%. | C |
| [2608.13040](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13040) | Latent On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/bingreeky/LOPD) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Compresses retrieved experience into continuous latent tokens that serve as privileged context for the self-teacher, learned end to end; the student gets dense supervision at every prefix of its self-generated trajectories. Result: Beats RLVR, OPSD, SDPO and Skill-SD on tool use and code generation with under 30% of the rollout budget. | CTHKX |
| [2608.13179](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13179) | Teach the Magnitude, Not the Direction: Verifier-Anchored Credit Assignment for Multi-Turn Multi-Step LLM Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | The sign of each token's advantage is anchored to the verifiable reward and its magnitude is modulated by a privileged self-teacher through entropy gating; advantages are split per turn to ease cross-turn credit dilution. Result: Beats RL and distillation baselines at both scales on BFCL V3 and WildToolBench, with the largest gains on long trajectories. | CT |
| [2608.13205](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13205) | HPSD: Hybrid-Policy Self-Distillation for Text-Image-to-Video Diffusion Models [code](https://github.com/Bujiazi/HPSD) | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | The same TI2V model acts as teacher with the first frame and an enhanced prompt and as student in pure T2V mode; the student locally pulls the teacher anchor back to its own policy, then receives velocity-level supervision on self-generated rollouts. Result: Significantly improves basic T2V generation ability, with clear gains in TI2V mode as well. | CTJ |
| [2608.13395](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13395) | FIRE-VLA: Failure-Informed Self-Evolution for Vision-Language-Action Models in Autonomous Driving | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Failure groups with low reward and low diversity trigger self-distillation: a frozen copy from the start of the round additionally sees the hidden future trajectory and supervises only answer tokens along the student prefix, while GRPO runs as usual on all groups. Result: At G=4, average L2 drops from 1.848 to 1.500 m and the persistent failure rate from 13.03% to 11.20%. | CJ |
| ★[2608.14144](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14144) | Self-Supervised Visual On-Policy Distillation [code](https://williamium3000.github.io/s2vopd) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Reverses the source of asymmetry: instead of giving the teacher privileges, it cuts the student's information, with the teacher seeing the original image and the student a strongly augmented view, and runs on-policy distillation on student trajectories. Result: On six fine-grained perception benchmarks Qwen3.5-4B rises from 70.7% to 77.4%, recovering 96% of the gain of privileged methods. | CTJK |
| [2608.14647](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14647) | SMOPD: Selective Token-Entropy Masking for Dirty-History Multi-Turn On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | In multi-turn dirty-history OPSD, ranks intermediate-turn responses by student entropy and drops the 20% lowest-entropy tokens before computing a clipped generalized JSD loss, with no extra parameters. Result: SHARDED accuracy rises by +1.0–2.5pp at three Qwen3 sizes, and by +1.7pp at 4B across multiple seeds (p=0.022). | CT |
| [2608.14684](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14684) | Mitigating Rubric Interference in LLM Judges via On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Uses the model's own single-rubric judgments as anchors and applies on-policy self-distillation to align its reasoning in single-pass multi-rubric evaluation, mitigating interference among rubrics. Result: Consistently improves evaluation consistency across three datasets and two model families, and the learned consistency transfers across datasets. | CTH |
| [2608.14945](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14945) | Trust Is Not Enough: Influence Calibration for On-Policy Self-Distillation in Agentic RL [code](https://github.com/lanqz7766/Influence-Calibration-for-On-Policy-Self-Distillation-in-Agentic-RL) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Identifies a trust-utility mismatch in allocating supervision by teacher confidence, and instead calibrates each token's distillation weight by the first-order response of an RL surrogate to perturbations in the teacher direction. Result: At 7B, 96.1% on ALFWorld and 93.1 on WebShop; anti-objective token mass 60.1%→37.8%. | CTH |
| [2608.20122](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20122) | ArmorOCR: Grounded Adversarial Visual Perception via Observation-Transferred Self-Distillation | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Two-stage training for adversarial OCR: OPSD from privileged transformed observations first fills in missing perception ability, then GRPO with task-conditioned rewards refines localization, recognition and VQA. Result: The accompanying AdvSpot benchmark has 390 images covering 13 adversarial OCR types in 5 major categories; performance on both adversarial and general OCR is maintained or improved. | TJ |
| [2608.21500](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21500) | SecOPD: Mitigating Adaptive Prompt Injections by On-Policy Distillation [code](https://github.com/pppyb/SecOPD) | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Prompt-injection defense: the model rolls out on injected samples, and the initial model scores each token given the corresponding clean input, replacing sequence-level feedback. Result: Qwen3.6-27B has an ASR of 9.0% against PISmith versus 94.0% for the previous SoTA; 4.7% on tool calling in unseen domains. | CTN |
| [2608.21946](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21946) | EDGE: Experience-Distillation for Guided Exploration in Agentic Reinforcement Learning [code](https://github.com/xvolcano02/EDGE) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Treats retrieved experience as temporary training-time scaffolding: estimates the positive marginal gain from experience-conditioned versus experience-free trajectories, then uses reverse KL on the support of the model's own experience to internalize the behavior into its parameters. Result: Improves by up to 12.5 points over strong RL baselines on embodied, web and search QA tasks, without needing the scaffolding at inference. | C |
| [2608.24114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24114) | AHEAD: Adaptive Hindsight with Environment-Augmented Distillation for Agentic RL | Agents | Same model, privileged | Step-wise hindsight distillation for multi-turn agentic RL: the teacher gets environment feedback at every step as a dense signal and, only at erroneous steps, an extra LLM-generated corrective hint, with minimal changes to GRPO. Result: At 7B, +13.3 points on ALFWorld and +11.0 on WebShop over GRPO, under a tighter interaction budget. | - |
| [2608.24646](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24646) | On-Policy Self-Distillation in Diffusion Models [code](https://github.com/worldbench/DiffusionOPSD) | Image, video and audio generation | Own earlier checkpoint | For diffusion models, turns image-level reward guidance into explicit targets for clean-output prediction: a frozen behavior policy samples trajectories and anchors, reward gradients build bounded positive and negative targets, followed by an EMA refresh. Result: Best in 19 of 20 settings across two backbones and ten evaluators, beating the strongest competitor by up to 44.0% and cutting GPU hours by 40–63%. | CJK |
| [2608.25356](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25356) | Where to Look Matters: On-Policy Self-Distillation for Long-Video Understanding | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Long-video understanding: a self-teacher is formed with the cue interval as a privileged condition, and next-token distributions are aligned on trajectories self-generated by the full-video student; inference needs no cue annotations or extra modules. Result: Consistently beats the backbone across multiple long-video benchmarks and all Qwen3.5 sizes, and is stronger than supervised post-training baselines. | CJ |
| [2608.26019](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26019) | DualOPSD: Closing the Feedback Loop in On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/on1262/DualOPSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | In OPSD the privileged teacher stays fixed; here the teacher also adapts after each student update, and a residual objective against the frozen initial model preserves the effect of the privileged context. Result: Across Qwen3-8B/4B/1.7B and three math benchmarks, avg@12 is 9.83 percentage points higher than OPSD. | CTH |
| [2608.27065](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27065) | Video-OPSD: Exploiting Privileged Visual Evidence for On-Policy Self-Distillation in Video Large Language Models | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | OPSD for video LLMs: the teacher sees only annotated evidence frames while the student sees the full video, and the distillation loss is adaptively weighted by how much each reasoning token depends on the privileged visual evidence. Result: Reliably beats standard OPSD on several backbones and is on par with GRPO with clearly less training time. | CJ |
| ★[2608.27448](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27448) | TTPO: Test-Time Policy Optimization [code](https://github.com/ZJU-REAL/TTPO) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Label-free test-time training: rollouts that agree with the majority vote go through OPSD distillation, disagreeing ones are penalized with group RL, and at the token level only confident errors are penalized. Result: Matches labeled OPSD on five competition-level benchmarks, with Qwen3-1.7B rising from 38.0% to 45.2%. | H |
| [2608.28306](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28306) | VISTA: Verifier-Informed Student-to-Teacher Adaptation for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Standard OPSD updates only the student; VISTA uses outcome-verified rollouts to pull the privileged teacher toward the student distribution, changing only the top-k positions with the largest teacher-student KL. Result: On AIME24/25 and HMMT25, Avg@12 at three scales is 0.6/0.7/2.1 points above OPSD. | C |
| [2608.29588](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29588) | Call Neighbours Yourself: Graph Walks with Destination-Conditioned On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/superallen13/CNY) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Lets an LLM actively walk edges on text-attributed graphs to gather evidence; once a neighbor's content is revealed, it looks back to assess the change in action preference brought by that choice and turns it into an action-level self-distillation signal to solve the delayed-credit problem. Result: Beats fixed-context post-training baselines on TAG reasoning benchmarks, and the policy transfers to unseen graphs and graph-level tasks. | - |
| [2609.03241](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03241) | FlowBalance: Verifier-Grounded Self-Improvement from On-Policy Reasoning Experience [code](https://github.com/alexhuang13/FlowBalance) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | A frozen view of the same model gives token-level log-prob gains from privileged context, which are aggregated into a trajectory-level self-guidance score; the verifier's group advantage then calibrates its direction, and it is fit with trajectory balance. Result: On math reasoning it beats FlowRL on average on Qwen3-4B/8B and avoids the response-length collapse of direct OPSD. | C |
| [2609.04203](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04203) | Temporal Self-Distillation: Learning Visual State Tracking in Videos Without Supervision | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Treats the temporal sampling density of video as privileged information: the same-weight model on a dense view acts as teacher, and the student on a sparse view matches its next-token distribution, with no labels, extra teacher or reward needed. Result: On LLaVA-OneVision-2-8B, VSTAT improves by +1.74, and by +2.38 after souping; +7.95 when transferred to real videos. | J |
| [2609.07036](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07036) | Aha-Flow Distillation: Flow Markers Matter in LLM Reasoning [code](https://github.com/Wang-Xiaodong1899/Aha-Flow-Distillation) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Identifies Flow markers as the counterpart of Aha markers and rewrites discourse markers to build Flow-CoT as dual-mode privileged information for OPSD, while inference uses only the standard reflection instruction. Result: Avg@12 rises from 60.8 to 61.3 on Qwen3-8B and from 57.5 to 58.6 on Qwen3-4B. | CT |
| [2609.09957](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09957) | Beyond Verified Answers: Solver-Informed Self-Distillation for Bootstrapping Operations Research Language Models | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Self-improvement for operations research modeling without verified answers: candidate programs from multiple rollouts are executed and their objective values clustered, the majority group's solver output serves as a pseudo reference, and updates combine group-relative advantages with dense self-supervision. Result: Solve accuracy on several operations research benchmarks beats group-relative training with outcome rewards alone, for both general and operations-research-finetuned models. | CH |
| [2609.11699](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11699) | Negative Self-Distillation: Learning to Reason by Avoiding Flaws [code](https://github.com/Prongcan/NSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Instead of imitating privileged solutions, the model builds its own "sloppy reasoner" negatively conditioned teacher and pushes the student distribution away from it, with dynamic gating that penalizes only reasoning-critical tokens. Result: Consistently better than OPSD and other label-free bootstrapping RL baselines. | C |
| [2609.12579](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12579) | SCOPE-OPSD: Fisher-Conditioned Privileged Subspaces for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | OPSD gains a second channel beyond next-token probabilities: the final-layer residuals of the privileged teacher and the student are projected onto a rank-64 frozen subspace estimated from the Fisher and the residual covariance for supervision. Result: Across 12 model-checkpoint combinations of Qwen3 1.7B/4B/8B, 11 beat plain OPSD and 10 beat the random control. | C |
| [2609.15177](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15177) | Temporal Self-Distillation: Faster Inference in Discrete Diffusion Language Models | Diffusion language models | Same model, privileged | On-policy self-distillation for diffusion language models: the denoising distribution at early timesteps is aligned with the distribution at the moment the token is finally committed, so early predictions anticipate the final output and support more aggressive parallel decoding. Result: Pushes the speed-quality frontier into the low-compute regime on seven benchmarks across math, planning and code, with speedup comparable to offline distillation in a single stage. | C |
| [2609.15296](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15296) | Reason What Matters: Retrieval-Grounded Reasoning for Universal Multimodal Embeddings | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Uses the input supporting evidence that separates positives from hard negatives as privileged guidance, with an on-policy self-teacher giving token-level feedback on student CoT trajectories, and learns a retrieval-aware early-stopping criterion. Result: Reaches SOTA on MMEB-V2 and MRMR, with inference throughput up to 5× that of explicit CoT methods. | J |
| [2609.15313](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15313) | Reducing the Output-Mode Gap in Speech Language Models via Joint-Output On-Policy Distillation | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Points out that the internal text accuracy of speech LLMs during interleaved generation is much lower than in text-only mode (the output-mode gap), and distills the stronger S2T policy into joint generation on student-generated interleaved trajectories with a non-text prediction retention term. Result: On Step-Audio-2-mini, the OMG on Spoken-MQA drops from 42.87 to 16.26 percentage points. | CJ |
| [2609.20784](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20784) | RetireOPD: Self-Retiring On-Policy Distillation for Agentic Reinforcement Learning | Agents | Same model, privileged | Notes that privileged information does not necessarily make the teacher reliable and that the teacher's benefit varies by stage: a decoupled skill-conditioned teacher is trained first, student RL and OPD run together, and the teacher is retired automatically once the gap stops shrinking. Result: Success rate improves by 14.1%-18.8% over the RL baseline on ALFWorld and by 11.8%-19.0% on WebShop. | CTHK |
| [2609.23038](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23038) | Spatial-Interactor: Learning Spatial Reasoning through Interaction with the Observable Physical World [code](https://github.com/ZJU-OmniAI/Spatial-Interactor) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Teaches a VLM physical state transitions from interaction trajectories: L1/L2 use SFT, while L3 long trajectories use privileged self-distillation in which the teacher branch sees segment-level transition descriptions and supervises the student's self-sampled CoT. Result: Builds the LSI-108K interaction dataset, with gains in both local transition and long-range integration across several VLMs and spatial benchmarks. | J |
| [2609.23449](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23449) | PSD: Pseudo Self-Distillation of Memory Representation Capabilities for LLM Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | One small model serves as both teacher and student to learn hierarchical memory construction: the teacher prompt embeds a black-box oracle's answer as reference context, while the student sees only the task prompt and fits the teacher's output distribution. Result: On LoCoMo, Qwen3 0.6B/1.7B/4B match or beat GPT-4.1-mini, and the off-policy variant is strongest under most conditions. | - |
| [2609.24646](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24646) | iSDFT: Information-Proximal Self-Distillation for Continual Learning in LLMs [code](https://github.com/KickItLikeShika/iSDFT) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Treats the demonstration-conditioned self-distillation teacher as a budgeted information source: for each token the target is the distribution closest to the student that still satisfies a teacher information constraint, anchored to the frozen base model to prevent drift. Result: Beats the original SDFT in 7 of 8 cases across four backbones and two task types, and 73% of evaluations stay within 0.5 points of the base model, against 52% for the strongest baseline. | - |
| ★[2609.25623](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25623) | What Should a Self-Teacher See? Privileged Context Design for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Systematically compares what privileged context the self-teacher in OPSD should see: the common full reference solution against three offline abstractions, a named strategy, a method-agnostic framework and the problem category, plus a control giving only the final answer. Result: The best intermediate abstraction is 1.4/1.6 points above the full solution at 4B/8B, with an order of magnitude fewer prompt tokens. | C |
| [2609.27307](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27307) | Learn How to Act from Your Own Interactions: On-Policy Self-Distillation for GUI Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Extends OPSD from GUI grounding to multi-turn interaction: privileged following is first strengthened by jointly optimizing rollouts with and without privilege from the same state, then reasoning and memory are distilled selectively. Result: Pass@1/Pass@3 on AndroidWorld and MobileWorld both beat the OPSD baselines evaluated. | CTJH |
| ★[2609.29051](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29051) | From Self-Distillation to Self-Practice: Privileged Information for Multi-Turn Agents [code](https://sux1ngyu.github.io/PSP/) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Points out that OPSD on multi-turn agents produces students that pretend to master privileged information they never saw, so privileged instructions are moved into the sampler while the loss stays vanilla GRPO. Result: All three students are best on AppWorld and SWE-bench Verified, improving 7%-58% over GRPO. | CTX |
| [2609.30652](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30652) | Recursive Self-Improvement via On-Policy Distillation for Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Breaks with the OPSD practice of keeping the privileged teacher frozen, lets the teacher and student co-evolve, and uses shorter verified rewrites to suppress verbose self-critique. Result: On Qwen3-8B, Average@12 reaches 65.97%, 35.62 percentage points above OPSD, and output length drops by 7.80%. | CH |
| [2609.30878](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30878) | TISD: On-Policy Self-Distillation with Trajectory Intervention | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Uses controlled token interventions to find that teacher-student divergence points are better for proposing trajectory branches than for local error correction, so it forces a teacher branch, has the student continue, and distills the whole trajectory. Result: On coding models, Avg@4 is 1.2 percentage points above SDPO; in the science domain at equal steps, Avg@128 is 0.8 points higher. | CH |
| ★[2609.32201](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32201) | Instruct, Not Answer: Using Instruction Privileges in On-Policy Context Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | In on-policy context distillation, replaces per-problem reference answers with a few general instructions written for the student's common mistakes, used as the teacher's privileged information. Result: In seven of eight experiments on ProverQA and other datasets, OOD scores are 4–17 points higher than using gold answers, while in-domain results are on par. | - |
| [2609.32333](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32333) | Progressive-View On-Policy Distillation for Regional-to-Global Transfer in Multimodal LLMs | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Uses aspect-ratio-preserving padded crops as intermediate views to move the student's view distribution gradually from crops to the full image, and weights token supervision by the log difference of the crop-conditioned teacher. Result: Average accuracy over three seeds is 77.51, 2.01 points above the reward-free distillation baseline and 1.00 point above the reward-matching variant. | CJ |
| [2609.32353](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32353) | Fewer Tokens, More Self-Teaching: On-Policy Self-Distillation for Extreme Visual Token Reduction [code](https://github.com/Yrxxxxxxxx1007/LT-OPD) | Quantization and pruning | Same model, privileged | An MLLM with an extremely low visual token budget samples its own responses, a full-token copy of the same model gives distribution supervision along these trajectories, and a budget curriculum compresses step by step. Result: With 5% of visual tokens kept, average retention rises from 68.6% to 82.3% and KV cache drops by 85.2%. | J |
| [2609.32472](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32472) | AdaTutoRank: Learning to Rerank Document Sets via Adaptive Tutoring Optimization for RAG and Deep Research [code](https://adatutorank.github.io/) | Other applications | Same model, privileged | In set reranking training, gives three tiers of hints by rollout quality, and uses the rescoring difference between a hint-conditioned frozen teacher and a hint-free snapshot as a token-level advantage that supplements the group-relative reward. Result: Best overall on ten benchmarks covering RAG, deep research and set evaluation, with fewer retrieval calls. | - |
| [2609.32667](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32667) | Learning from a Thoughtful Teacher: Adaptive On-Policy Self-Distillation for Mathematical Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Finds that a few high-divergence tokens dominate the OPSD signal and easily leak the privileged answer, so it encodes the solution as a reasoning DAG and releases subgraphs step by step as privileged information according to the student's ability. Result: Pass@8 reaches 72.5% on HMMT25/AIME24/25/BRUMo25, 6.7 points above OPSD and 4.2 points above the strongest baseline. | C |
| [2609.32690](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32690) | MM-OPD: Towards One More Bottleneck Between Perception and Reasoning [code](https://github.com/TungChintao/MM-OPD) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Finds a "symbolic-visual gap" in which symbolic-view inputs are actually more accurate, uses symbol-conditioned behavior as the privileged teacher, and corrects the image-conditioned policy's evidence selection through on-policy self-distillation with a residual token-level objective. Result: Replacing images with symbolic views actually raises performance by 10.2%~23.6%; perception, charts and documents, math and VQA all improve. | J |
| [2609.32694](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32694) | IGSD: Environment-Verified Hindsight Self-Distillation for Search Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Hindsight self-distillation for search agents: completes the teacher and student candidate query tokens and actually executes them with the same retriever, then uses the executable paired information gain to put positive weights on candidate-pair distillation. Result: On seven single-hop/multi-hop QA tasks, the 3B and 7B policies reach macro-average EM of 42.8% and 47.0% respectively. | C |
| [2609.33181](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33181) | SeOPD: Self-Evolving LLMs via Online Policy Distillation from Self-Generated Chain-of-Thought | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Needs no external privileged information: uses CoT generated in deep-thinking mode as privileged information to give token-level supervision to the same model's answers in non-thinking mode, internalizing new information into the shared parameters. Result: Effective across multiple models and tasks, improving both non-thinking and deep-thinking abilities. | - |
| [2609.33200](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33200) | Teach Yourself Where to Look: On-Policy Attention Self-Distillation for Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Adds attention distillation on top of token-level OPSD: the attention of a privileged teacher that can see the verified solution is projected onto positions visible to the student, renormalized, and then aligned. Result: Across three scales and four math benchmarks, 4.98~8.40 points above pure token OPSD, with 73.9% fewer rollout tokens. | CH |
| [2609.33391](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33391) | Beyond Timestamps: Decision-Aligned On-Policy Distillation for Long-Horizon Agents [code](https://github.com/mingju-c/Align-OPSD) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Points out the decision-timestamp mismatch in OPSD: calibrates teacher evidence by rescoring in functionally matched contexts from sibling rollouts, then assigns credit to variable-length decision segments with semi-Markov hierarchical credit assignment. Result: Beats GRPO and StepOPSD across all eight comparisons on ALFWorld/WebShop/Search-QA. | C |
| [2609.34009](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34009) | Fisher-Informed Recalibration for Feedback-Based On-Policy Self-Distillation of LLMs | General LLM | Same model, privileged | FIRE, a dual-branch method for feedback-based self-distillation, which is prone to collapse: correct answers switch to reweighted on-policy SFT, wrong answers get recalibrated feedback targets, and the step size is controlled by a radius derived from the Fisher trace. Result: Significantly more stable in settings where standard feedback-conditioned distillation is unstable, while keeping downstream performance. | C |
| [2609.34044](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34044) | SCOPD: Sparse-Context On-Policy Self-Distillation for Efficient Vision-Language Models [code](https://github.com/Enmingzz/scopd-repo) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Proposes the "representation-utilization gap": the student generates trajectories with pruned visual tokens while a full-context privileged teacher supervises the same on-policy prefix; SCOPD+ further selects visually sensitive positions for distillation. Result: With 10% of visual tokens kept, average retention over 13 benchmarks goes 86.37%→90.49%, and the + version reaches 92.43%. | J |
| [2609.34082](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34082) | K-OPSD: Verifiable On-Policy Self-Distillation for Post-Training Vision-Language Models on AEC Drawings | Other applications | Same model, privileged | OPSD post-training for understanding architectural drawings: a process-level verifier certifies the model's own best-of-N as the teacher, failed samples are resampled under prompts that reveal the answer, and updates use an inner cross-entropy loss. Result: On AECV-Bench, the average judge score is 0.819 and overall accuracy 0.738; cross-entropy beats bounded token-level JSD. | J |
| [2609.34418](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34418) | OSPD: On-Policy Self-Distillation for Persona-Consistent Dialogue | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Same-model self-distillation: the teacher gets the full persona profile while the student sees only a brief summary; along student trajectories it switches divergences by the bimodal confidence of role-critical tokens, and progressively masks persona features. Result: Beats SFT and multi-turn RL baselines on persona consistency across three benchmarks including CharacterBench. | - |
| [2609.34519](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34519) | EOPSA: Efficient On-Policy Self-Distilled Safety Alignment | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Diagnoses long-rollout supervision collapse and style-gradient dilution in safety OPSD, adaptively truncates generation by the teacher rescue rate, and distills only safety-critical tokens. Result: On reasoning models up to 32B, rollout compute drops by about 50% and only about 2% of tokens are backpropagated, while still beating full-token distillation. | C |
| [2609.34672](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34672) | Evidence-Aligned Multimodal On-Policy Self-Distillation for Fine-Grained Visual Understanding | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Keeps the crop-conditioned teacher as the target, but builds an evidence reference from the current student's prediction difference between the original image and an evidence-masked image, weights teacher corrections by cosine alignment, and removes interference from stale model states and crop context. Result: Keeping only about 6% of the supervision of dense OPSD, it still consistently beats the previous best method. | CJ |
| [2609.34810](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34810) | UniOPSD: Unifying Outcome and Hindsight Feedback for Agentic Reinforcement Learning [code](https://github.com/Zenghuang-Fu/Uniopsd) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Turns environment returns and privileged hindsight feedback into comparable credit estimates at shared interaction anchors, setting the global mixing ratio by historical consistency and arbitrating locally by signal availability and relative precision. Result: ALFWorld 82.8/83.6%, WebShop 75.0/82.0%; +7.0 points over SDAR at 3B. | C |
| [2609.34848](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34848) | Can We Trust the Teacher? Decoupled Credit Direction-Magnitude for Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Decouples the direction and magnitude of step-level credit into two reliable signals, belief-margin probing for direction and marginal information gain for magnitude, used to calibrate privileged-teacher supervision and to assign credit from steps to tokens. Result: Best overall on 11 benchmarks, +8.45 points on math reasoning and +7.01 points on multimodal reasoning over the base model, and the credit direction of 6% of tokens is corrected. | C |
| [2609.34863](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34863) | Revisit to Segment: Working Memory Distillation for Reasoning Segmentation | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Selects its own rollouts by segmentation quality as working memory to condition a teacher, gives token-level distribution supervision along student trajectories, and jointly optimizes reasoning segmentation with outcome-driven RL. Result: Reaches state of the art on multiple reasoning segmentation benchmarks (the abstract gives no specific numbers). | J |
| [2609.35262](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35262) | Rubric-Aware On-Policy Self-Distillation for LLM Personalization [code](https://github.com/SnowCharmQ/GRASP) | Other applications | Same model, privileged | The teacher additionally sees the user's rubric while the student does not, and next-token distributions are aligned along student trajectories, turning rubric requirements into dense token-level supervision. Result: Reaches state of the art on LaMP-QA personalized question answering across multiple backbone models. | C |
| [2609.35303](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35303) | PIVOT: Pivot-Aware On Policy Self Distillation for Multi-Turn VLM Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Uses counterfactual rollback probes to find that OPSD gains come mainly from state rollback at pivot steps, so it internalizes pivot localization and state recovery into token-level updates. Result: Qwen2.5-VL-3B reaches 0.90 accuracy, +8% over SFT+GRPO; Qwen3-VL-2B reaches 0.92. | CJ |
| [2609.35540](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35540) | Continuous Context Management | Agents | Same model, privileged | The agent compresses its context every turn, and on top of GRPO, a frozen copy of the student's initial model scores the agent's own rollout actions under the full history as dense supervision. Result: On WebShop both scales beat GRPO; Qwen3-4B surpasses full-history GRPO, while 8B does not. | - |
| [2609.35611](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35611) | On-Policy Self-Distillation for Multi-Turn Image Editing [code](https://github.com/liangbingzhao/MT-OPSD) | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | In multi-turn image editing, the student generates conditioned on its own imperfect images while a same-model teacher conditioned on clean images provides editing supervision, with no multi-turn annotation needed. Result: Long-horizon editing success rates rise clearly on three editing backbones while single-turn quality is largely preserved; also releases LME-Bench (100 ten-turn sessions). | CJ |
| [2609.36364](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36364) | Compress to Remember: Learning Compact Memory via On-Policy Distillation for Long Video Generation | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | The same frozen video generator acts as the student when conditioned on compressed memory and as the teacher when conditioned on the full history, the two are aligned on the same noisy input from the student's continuation, and only the compressor is updated. Result: On MBench, Causal-rCM beats the strongest baseline by 6.63 points, and Causal Forcing by 3.19 points. | - |
| [2609.36435](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36435) | MemFold: Learning Compact Soft Memory for Long-Context Personalization via On-Policy Optimization [code](https://github.com/Johnny221B/memfold) | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Compresses text memory into a fixed number of soft vectors, and on its own rollouts combines group-relative rewards with rescoring by a confidence-gated text-memory teacher; the teacher is never sampled from. Result: Three Qwen backbones reach the highest accuracy on PersonaMem-32K/128K, with a larger advantage on long histories. | - |
| [2609.36638](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36638) | PE-OPSD: Internalizing Prompt Enhancement into Flow-matching Models via On-Policy Self-Distillation | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | Treats the enhanced prompt as privileged information: a raw-prompt student samples along its own generation trajectory while an enhanced-prompt teacher gives vector-field targets at those states, and the prompt rewriter is removed at inference. Result: Across multiple model families, prompt enhancers, and benchmarks, achieves the strongest aggregate prompt fidelity while keeping the base model's inference efficiency. | - |
| [2609.36642](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36642) | PR-OPD: Privileged Representation On-policy Self-Distillation for Agentic Reinforcement Learning [code](https://github.com/balibata/PR-OPD) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Points out that skill privilege changes token probabilities very little but hidden states a lot, and instead aligns the projected hidden states layer by layer to a privileged self-teacher, jointly optimized with the reward objective. Result: A skill lifts WebShop from 42.2% to 56.2% yet changes fewer than 1/4 of token probabilities; up to +14.0 over GRPO. | CH |
| [2609.36679](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36679) | MLToolBench: Learning Tool-Augmented Agents for Machine Learning Development | Agents | Same model, privileged | A tool suite and training pipeline for machine learning engineering agents; SPICE uses how privileged context changes the likelihood of a sampled tool action as a turn-level reward to supplement the final outcome. Result: In-domain Qwen3-8B 24.8% to 52.4%, 35B-A3B 35.6% to 69.2%, out-of-domain 31% to 48%. | N |
| [2609.36695](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36695) | Know Thyself, Teach Thyself: Internal Information Flow for Selective Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/1240148048/INFLOW) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Retrieval-augmented on-policy self-distillation: retrieves information sources with certainty-calibrated hidden-state trajectories, then selects samples by the JS divergence between the teacher's answer beliefs before and after retrieval before distilling. Result: Across four open-weight models and three knowledge domains, achieves the strongest cross-model average among the compared selection methods. | H |
| [2609.36803](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36803) | EGSD: Event-Grounded Self-Distillation for Streaming Video Understanding | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Points out that question-plus-answer privilege in streaming video causes teacher-student preference misalignment and effective-entity memory collapse, and instead uses events as privilege to reweight the teacher and adds an entity-coverage reward. Result: StreamingBench 79.8%, OVO-Bench Real-Time track 73.4%; effective-entity recall +17.4%. | C |
| [2609.37044](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37044) | Learning from Think-Mode Advantage via On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Treats think mode as privileged information for OPD, proposes trace-response divergence (TRD), and combines it with group-relative reward gain to route supervision weights at the response level with within-group normalization. Result: Outperforms Uniform ThinkOPD on both same-model and two cross-model teacher-student pairs. | C |
| [2609.37132](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37132) | Train Ahead, Distill Back: Bootstrapping On-Policy Self-Distillation for Large Language Models | General LLM | Own earlier checkpoint | Temporarily trains the policy ahead to obtain a future teacher, then restores the student to its original state and has that future teacher supervise the restarted on-policy trajectories. Result: In the rollout-privileged setting, Qwen3-4B 27.50 to 41.30 and Qwen3-8B 48.80 to 64.44. | CH |
| [2609.37837](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37837) | Can Vision-Language Models Stay Helpful When Facing Implicit Risks? Intent-Privilege OPSD for Efficient Safety-Helpfulness Alignment | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Gives the teacher evidence-grounded intent as training-time privileged supervision and self-distills with only one rollout per prompt, so the VLM recognizes cross-modal implicit risks rather than refusing wholesale. Result: With only 1447 safety samples (95% fewer), training time is cut to 1/5, and the safe-and-helpful ratio on SIUO+HoliSafe rises from 43.9% to 53.5%. | C |
| [2609.37915](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37915) | Overcoming Scaling Limits in On-Policy Self-Distillation for LLM Reasoning | General LLM | Same model, privileged | A factorial analysis separates scaffold correctness from teacher-context correctness and finds the former more important; OASIS supervises only verified trajectories and uses the student's own failed attempts as teacher context. Result: The OPSD gain drops from 3.05 points at 1.7B to 0.14 at 8B; OASIS is 3.05 points above OPSD at 8B. | C |
| [2609.38072](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38072) | RS-OPSD: Reliable Privileged On-Policy-Self-Distillation for Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing VQA | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Internalizes the zoom-in-for-evidence visual privilege of ultra-high-resolution remote sensing VQA: builds a dataset with evidence-region annotations and uses context-preserving visual privilege and correctness alignment to mitigate unreliable teachers. Result: On XLRS-Bench and two other benchmarks, exceeds same-scale SOTA by 4.0 percentage points on average, and the 2B version surpasses most 8B-scale models. | - |
| [2609.38093](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38093) | Character Training for Risk-Averse Agents | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Writes a model constitution describing constant absolute risk aversion over resources and instills this character into the agent via on-policy distillation, so misaligned agents prefer dealing with humans over taking risks. Result: Despite not seeing the benchmark's decision format during training, it is competitive with baselines trained directly on it, and generalizes better out of distribution on 2 of 4 models. | - |
| [2609.38142](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38142) | AdviSD: Learning to Advise Frontier LLMs via Targeted Multi-Turn Self-Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | A small advisor model writes advice for a frozen frontier executor: it combines outcome-based RL with self-distillation from a feedback-conditioned copy of itself, and filters which corrections to learn by whether the advice actually changed the execution outcome. Result: A Qwen3-8B advisor beats advisor-GRPO by 4.2-6.4 points on BFCL-v3 and by 3.9-5.1 points on EnvScaler. | C |
| [2609.38146](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38146) | LIFT: Layout-In-Future Video Generation under Large Viewpoint Change via On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://jsxzs.github.io/LIFT/) | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | In image-to-video, uses the last-frame layout as an explicit control signal for the future viewpoint, and uses OPSD to transfer the control ability of a dense per-frame layout teacher to a student that only sees the last-frame layout. Result: Outperforms competing methods on video quality, future-layout controllability, and camera controllability. | C |
| [2609.38342](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38342) | Activation-Conditioned Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Extracts a steering vector by contrasting the activations of verified-correct versus the remaining self-generated trajectories; the frozen base model plus this vector acts as teacher and gives per-token distribution supervision on student prefixes, without reference-solution text. Result: Highest average across five models and four math benchmarks; at 8B, math 71.9% and LCB v6 70.9%, above reference-conditioned OPSD. | C |
| [2609.38721](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38721) | UniEvo-VL: An On-policy Self-Distillation Training Recipe for Multimodal Model Self-improvement | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | A unified multimodal model treats self-critique as privileged information: the same model sees the critique as teacher and only the original question as student, minimizing the per-state divergence of their denoising distributions along the student's own sampled trajectory. Result: On Qwen-image-2512, GenEval 0.747 to 0.808 and GenEval2 Soft-TIFA 32.97 to 35.53. | C |
| [2609.38792](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38792) | Training LLM Judges from Language Feedback via Position-Selective Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/horizon-llm/OPSD-GenRM) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Uses the same model conditioned on natural-language feedback as teacher to train an LLM judge, distinguishing context sharpening from spreading by the per-position entropy shift between teacher and student, and supervising only the low-tail positions of the entropy shift. Result: Masking high-entropy-shift positions improves out-of-distribution generalization; the self-distilled judge is 2-9 percentage points above outcome-supervised RL on subjective subcategories. | C |
| [2609.39021](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39021) | Frame Differential On-Policy Self-Distillation for Video Reasoning | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | In video-reasoning RL, compares the token-level preferences of sparse versus dense frame views for the same sampled response and self-distills the frame-differential signal into the sparse-frame policy, with no external teacher and unchanged inference. Result: On two VLMs across six video-reasoning benchmarks and 16/32/64-frame settings, outperforms GRPO/T-GRPO/Video-KTR overall. | C |
| [2609.39150](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39150) | OP-CAD: On-Policy Clean-Audio Distillation for Robust Audio-Visual Reasoning | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | The student answers under noisy audio-visual input while a frozen teacher supervises the same prefix with clean audio and the verified answer, weighting tokens by prediction sensitivity across the three contexts. Result: Outperforms competing methods under all evaluated noise conditions, better preserves correct answers on clean samples under strong interference, and has no overall clean-accuracy drop. | C |
| [2609.39306](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39306) | ReSAIL: Mitigating Collapse in Iterative Agent Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/ShengjieJin/ReSAIL) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Against cross-cycle collapse in iterative privileged self-distillation, distills at steps where privileged information most changes the teacher's predictions and regularizes the student's privileged-conditioned distribution toward the frozen teacher to preserve next-cycle supervision. Result: On ALFWorld and TextCraft over three iterations, the final-cycle success rate improves by an average absolute 22.5% over self-distillation baselines. | C |
| [2609.39490](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39490) | OmniReasoning: Pushing the Limits of Audio-Visual Joint Reasoning [code](https://github.com/PKU-VaLuE-Lab/OmniReasoning) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Introduces an audio-visual joint-reasoning benchmark and data engine, and proposes modality-factored self-distillation: evaluating a sampled response under modality-specific clue contexts for token-level credit assignment. Result: +12.8 and +9.3 points over the base model on OmniVideoBench and OmniReasoningBench. | - |
| [2609.39884](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39884) | OPSRD: On-Policy Self-Role Distillation [code](https://github.com/zhansan114514/OPSRD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Uses a fixed expert role prompt as privileged context without reference solutions; the same base model's role-conditioned distribution gives teacher-weighted forward KL on the role-free student's prefixes, supervising only the highest-entropy half of positions. Result: On three competition-math benchmarks, beats the base model without role prompts; forward KL gives the highest macro-average at every scale. | C |
| [2609.39953](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39953) | Learning to Reason with Compressed Context: Ground-Truth-Free Adaptation of OmniLLMs via Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/Bamboos2003/CAFD) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Treats the full-token view of a sample as privileged information: a full-context self-teacher gives soft targets on the compressed-context student's on-policy trajectory, with no answers or rewards needed. Result: Improves 120 of 125 conditions, by +1.44 points on average, recovering 26.9% of the accuracy gap on average. | - |
| [2609.40253](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40253) | ComputerSD: Online Self-Distillation from Real-Time Feedback for Computer-Use Agents [code](https://github.com/ZJU-REAL/ComputerSD) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Turns real-time feedback from GUI execution into online self-distillation guidance: an analyzer gives per-step guidance as privileged context and a value score to modulate OPSD, jointly and asynchronously optimized with GRPO. Result: On OSWorld-Verified, two 8B backbones beat outcome-only GRPO by +1.9 and +4.1 points. | C |
| [2610.00757](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00757) | Video Evidence Indexing: Learning Where to Look from Video Previews for Token-Budgeted Long-Video Question Answering | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Long-video QA first views a low-resolution preview then picks high-resolution evidence frames within a token budget; lacking frame-level labels, an answer-aware teacher does privileged self-distillation on the student's generated retrieval traces. Result: Improves accuracy under limited visual budgets, and self-distillation further improves both QA accuracy and temporal evidence localization. | - |
| [2610.00979](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00979) | RISED: RubrIcs for agentic multi-environment Selection and sElf-Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | Tags multi-environment rollouts with a shared rubric: positive rubrics serve as privileged context for a self-distillation teacher, while negative rubrics guide sampling and data selection. Result: Achieves the highest cross-environment mean pass rate across multiple backbones and ranks first or second in every individual environment. | C |
| [2610.02117](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02117) | Where-OPD: Spatially Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation of MLLMs with Synthetic Scenes [code](https://github.com/sirkosophia/Where-OPD) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Uses procedurally generated scenes to automatically obtain object identities and coordinates as textual spatial guidance for the teacher, while the student reproduces the behavior from the image and question alone, giving annotation-free post-training. Result: Post-training on synthetic scenes alone transfers to real perception benchmarks, with an average +3.23-point gain across six benchmarks including CVBench, V*, and BLINK. | C |
| [2610.02191](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02191) | The Missing Primitive: Diagnosing and Repairing Mathematical Reasoning in Large Language Models | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Proposes a mathematical-primitive benchmark diagnosing structured math understanding along discovery, generation, digestion, and execution, identifies discovery as the main bottleneck, and uses primitives as privileged information for self-distillation to selectively transfer primitive-guided reasoning. Result: Consistently improves mathematical reasoning across model scales and hard benchmarks. | - |
| [2610.02700](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02700) | Learning from Evolving Errors: Adaptive Iterative Repair for On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Iteratively generates repair guidance for failed responses as privileged teacher context; the student retries with the guidance, and the teacher supervises only the error-aligned segment with stage weighting. Result: On Qwen3-4B and 8B, achieves the highest math-reasoning averages, up to 3.6 points above the strongest baseline. | C |
| [2610.03515](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03515) | Learning from Repaired Reasoning: Root-Cause-Guided On-Policy Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Locates the student's earliest substantive error and repairs it locally, using the repaired intermediate result as an anchor: the erroneous segment is distilled with root-cause guidance and the valid prefix with anchor guidance. Result: Experiments across multiple datasets and scales show that it mitigates reasoning mismatch and distillation traps with clear gains (no specific numbers given). | C |
| [2610.05048](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05048) | E²-OPSD: Taming Entropy Overshoot in On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/orionfei/E2-OPSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Identifies entropy overshoot in OPSD, where student entropy stays above the teacher's for long periods; it instead teaches with retrieved, already-solved neighboring problems as demonstrations, and uses the teacher-student entropy gap to set each token's correction direction and strength. Result: On math reasoning, mean@16 is up to +4.3 points over OPSD, and out of domain up to +4.9 points over the base model. | C |
| [2610.05070](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05070) | Outcome-Guided On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Revisits the OPSD objective from the advantage perspective of RLVR, dynamically adjusting the divergence form and the distillation positions by outcome correctness and the teacher's cumulative average entropy. Result: On Qwen3 1.7B/4B/8B and Qwen3-VL-2B, it beats the original OPSD on math, multimodal and out-of-domain tasks. | CTH |
| [2610.05303](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05303) | ASCENT: Online Test-Time Training of Long-Horizon Agents via Self-Distillation of Verified Experience [code](https://artificer-ai-lab.github.io/ASCENT) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Online self-distillation at deployment time: a frozen initial copy predicts distributions with verified trajectories as privileged information, which are distilled into LoRA fast weights, and invalid-action turns are filtered out. Result: On ALFWorld, WebShop and AppWorld, success rate and interaction efficiency rise as experience accumulates, and it transfers to unseen scenarios. | C |
| [2610.06342](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06342) | MeSD: Multi-Evidence Self-Distillation for VideoLLM | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Multi-evidence self-distillation for VideoLLMs: builds three evidence-conditioned teachers (answer, temporal, spatial) with shared parameters, fuses consensus with a gated residual, and uses successful and failed trajectories differently based on verification results. Result: Consistently beats RL and self-distillation baselines on multiple video benchmarks. | C |
| [2610.06401](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06401) | RAISED: Self-Distillation for Robustness to Prompt Injection in LLM Agents | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Defends against indirect prompt injection: the model first self-generates tool-use scenarios, then self-distillation makes the student match the teacher's clean-context behavior on both the clean and the injected variants. Result: Significantly lowers the success rate of prompt injections in tool returns while preserving utility on agent and general benchmarks. | C |
| [2610.06496](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06496) | You Changed Your Mind, The Model Didn't: Demystifying Intent in Multi-Turn Dialogue [code](https://github.com/junle-chen/intent) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Shows that models treat requests that were rejected or superseded as still in effect; proposes decision-conditioned OPSD, in which a frozen teacher gives active-intent supervision from the complete task under the user's final intent. Result: Intent-Eval shows that tool, code, database and math tasks are all affected by rejected proposals, increasingly so over more turns. | - |
| [2610.09484](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09484) | MIMESIS: Learning User Simulators as Training Environments for Interactive Agents [code](https://viethoang1512.github.io/mimesis/) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Trains high-fidelity user simulators as training environments and proposes coach-style on-policy self-distillation: a coach turns the simulator's private reasoning and subsequent utterances into notes, which become token-level dense supervision. Result: The 9B simulator scores 65.7 on SOUL-Index; agents trained with it outperform those trained with GPT-5.5 on all nine unseen user simulators. | N |
| [2610.09795](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09795) | MIRROR: From Imitation to Internalization in LLM Personalization | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Self-distillation for personalization: on the model's own generated trajectories, aligns to its own next-token distribution under reference conditioning to internalize preferences, and uses a focus plug-in to selectively supervise informative reference tokens. Result: Best overall on three personalized generation benchmarks at two scales, with less forgetting than SFT baselines. | N |
| [2610.10623](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10623) | Recurrent Self-Improvement: Dynamic Cross-Loop On-Policy Distillation for Looped Language Models | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Self-distillation across loop depths inside looped language models: the frozen final-loop policy serves as a compute-privileged teacher, giving dense supervision on trajectories self-sampled by the intermediate-loop student, and the teacher is refreshed dynamically. Result: Improves math reasoning on Ouro-Thinking, D-LoopOPD adds further gains, and it generalizes to general reasoning and code. | N |
| [2610.11366](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11366) | SpatialOPSD: Self-Distilling Spatial Intelligence from Verified Coding Agent Traces | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Feeds verified execution traces of a spatial coding agent to a same-model teacher as privileged context, self-distilling a tool-free spatial-reasoning MLLM while suppressing privileged information leakage. Result: Higher average accuracy than SFT and GRPO on multiple spatial and OOD benchmarks. | N |
| [2610.11384](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11384) | Environmental Feedback Modeling Matters: Rethinking Feedback Treatment in Agentic Hindsight Self-Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | Reward-free agentic hindsight self-distillation: argues that using feedback only as a teacher condition is not enough, and jointly optimizes environment feedback modeling and distillation from the feedback-conditioned self-teacher, which reinforce each other. Result: On Qwen3-8B, τ-bench success rate is 6.4/4.1 points above SDPO/GRPO, and AppWorld 10.71/3.57 points above. | TN |
| [2610.11529](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11529) | ReTeach: Building a Self-Teacher through Multi-Round Reflection and Retry | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Builds a self-teacher from only self-generated attempts and outcome-level verification: from failed rollouts it alternates reflection and retry until success, then runs on-policy distillation weighted by three outcome types. Result: Average accuracy is 1.39 percentage points above GRPO on six benchmarks in math, science QA and tool use. | NK |
| [2610.12355](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12355) | Distilling Routed 3D Privilege for Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models [code](https://github.com/ZJU-REAL/GPD) | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Renders depth, semantic and BEV geometric cues as text and routes them by question to a same-model teacher as privileged information, augmenting GRPO with a privileged KL applied only to incorrect trajectories; at deployment the student still uses only RGB. Result: On a 4B backbone, it reaches 57.1 on VSI-Bench and a four-benchmark average of 37.6, beating GRPO and OPSD with answer-only privilege. | N |
| [2610.13453](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13453) | Reflect-VLA: Internalizing Reflective Guidance via Dual-Path On-Policy Self-Distillation for Autonomous Driving | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Uses reflective diagnoses and corrective instructions as training-time privileged information and routes student trajectories by driving score: high-risk trajectories distill the teacher's token-level corrective distribution, reliable ones do self-imitation, and inference is a single forward pass with no reflection. Result: Competitive planning performance on NAVSIM v1/v2 and Bench2Drive, with training time over 90% lower than RL. | - |
| [2610.14001](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14001) | PreSO: Privileged Representation Isolation for On-Policy Self-Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Studies which parts of privileged information are worth distilling: representation shifts induced by compact hints are more consistent across problems, so hints serve as the privileged source, and the correction is projected onto a low-rank subspace before being added back to the student representation. Result: On five competition math benchmarks, Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B improve over the base by 6.8/9.0/10.0 points on average, and the final checkpoint also scores highest. | - |
| [2610.14763](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14763) | CUSE: Fine-Grained Multimodal Unlearning with Counterfactual Guidance and On-Policy Self-Distillation | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Multimodal unlearning: probability changes under counterfactual inputs locate the tokens to forget; then, on answers sampled from the current policy, the original model plus forget/retain privileged context serves as the teacher for distillation. Result: On MLLMU-Bench, forget-set ROUGE-L drops by 33.6%, and retain-set ROUGE-L is 1.2% higher than the strongest baseline. | - |
| [2610.16354](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16354) | Self-Distillation with Verbal Regularization [code](https://github.com/lijiazheng99/VRSD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Points out that privileged information biases the OPSD teacher toward shortcut reasoning with little verification and makes entropy drop sharply; uses a system prompt to adjust how the teacher uses privileged information so as to encourage rethinking, and gives theoretical conditions for entropy preservation. Result: Pass@1 beats OPSD in 11 of 12 settings (three models, four tasks), by 3.9 percentage points on average, and token entropy is higher overall. | - |
| [2610.17593](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17593) | Training Generalist Navigation Policies via Action Self-Distillation | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Navigation self-distillation: the teacher, the same VLM, uses privileged trajectory feedback to infer back the navigation goal and reasoning, and the student predicts trajectories from onboard observations alone, conditioned on that goal, without human annotation. Result: On 13 real-world indoor and outdoor tasks, success rates on indoor and multi-stage tasks are 30% higher than the strongest baseline, and outdoor success reaches 95%. | - |
| [2610.17734](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17734) | Explore with Memory, Learn without It: In-Context Exploration and Self-Distillation for LLM Reasoning [code](https://github.com/on1262/LEAF) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Before each rollout, injects a problem-level memory containing recent answers, rewards and feedback to speed up exploration, then writes the results back into the memory-free policy via self-distillation, decoupling updates from rollouts. Result: Across four benchmarks and two models, exploration is about 4 times as fast as the random-sampling baseline, accuracy on unseen test sets is 1.59/1.83 points above the strongest baseline, and compute is lower than every RL baseline. | - |
| [2610.17838](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17838) | Beyond Fixed Targets: On-Policy One-Shot Speaker Personalization for Autoregressive Text-To-Speech Models | Other applications | Same model, privileged | One-shot speaker personalization: identifies fixed off-policy supervision as the main cause of one-shot SFT failure, and switches to GRPO with a similarity reward and to OPSD with a teacher conditioned on the reference audio. Result: Both improve speaker similarity while preserving intelligibility; GRPO is best overall, while OPSD adapts much faster and needs no external reward evaluation. | - |
| [2610.18162](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18162) | LoOPSD: On-Policy Self-Distillation with Looped Teachers | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Builds a stronger self-teacher by searching the order in which layers are repeatedly executed, without changing weights; distills on student-generated prefixes, and alternates search and distillation to bootstrap round by round. Result: With a 400-token thinking budget on GSM8K, Qwen3-1.7B rises from 42.30% to 79.61%. | - |
| [2610.18256](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18256) | Teach Me After You Learn It: Lookahead Self-Distillation for RLVR | General LLM | Own earlier checkpoint | Performs virtual RL updates on disjoint subsets of on-policy rollouts to obtain lookahead teachers, which provide each other with cross-group token-level supervision and harmonize it with GRPO advantages. Result: Best in-domain average at every model scale, with out-of-domain generalization on par with GRPO. | - |
| [2610.19687](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19687) | Function-Space Temporal Self-Distillation for Policy Optimization | General LLM | Same model, privileged | A single-policy framework that combines the current privileged self-teacher with function-space temporal integration, storing sparse historical next-token distributions as fixed targets so that no resident teacher needs to be maintained. Result: Under a 5-hour training budget, average accuracy is about 4 points above the strongest baseline, and peak GPU memory drops from about 70GB to 56GB. | - |
| [2610.20516](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20516) | VASPO: Self-Distillation with Privileged Value Estimation [code](https://github.com/cii030/VASPO) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Lets the privileged self-teacher and the critic share the same privileged information on student prefixes: the critic produces token-level PPO advantages, and the self-teacher modulates positive advantages with a bounded probability ratio. Result: On six math benchmarks, improves over PPO by 2.59 and 1.81 percentage points on Qwen3-4B and OLMo3-7B, respectively. | - |
| [2610.21496](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21496) | OPRD: On-Policy Reminder Distillation for Long-Horizon Coding Agents | Agents | Same model, privileged | Routes reminder-free student rollouts by outcome: successful ones are used for self-imitation, and failed ones get reverse KL supervision on the same prefix from a teacher that sees multi-scale reminders. Result: The average resolve rate across three SWE-bench versions improves by 11.3 points over the Qwen3.5-4B base, and error events go 446→222. | - |
| ★[2610.22080](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22080) | Learning from Unverified Peers via Shared Residual Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Self-distillation without verification signals: compares how multiple unverified peer solutions to the same problem change the predicted distribution relative to a control, and keeps as supervision only adjustments that are consistent in direction and conservative in magnitude. Result: Qwen3-8B's AIME24-26 avg@12 rises from 21.39 to 54.44. | - |
| [2610.22408](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22408) | ECHOES: Seeing Beyond the Current Image via Recursive History Compression and Privileged On-Policy Distillation | Agents | Same model, privileged | A GUI agent recursively updates its text history in a compress-think-act format; the same model, given privileged multi-image input, acts as the teacher for dense token-level distillation on student trajectories, dynamically balanced with the task reward. Result: Both task performance and memory fidelity improve on four GUI evaluations and an in-house diagnostic set, and deployment does not need to keep historical screenshots. | - |
| [2610.22512](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22512) | Predicting Privileged Information Effectiveness for On-Policy Self-Distillation [code](https://github.com/zkjzou/PI-OPSD) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Systematically evaluates 16 privileged-information configurations on three multi-turn agent benchmarks and three students, and uses two KL metrics to predict before training which privileged information works better. Result: Selecting privileged information by the error-step KL contrast metric beats random selection, and combined with GRPO it still surpasses a strong GRPO baseline. | - |
| [2610.23185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23185) | A Stronger Teacher Within: Strengthening MoE Models via On-Policy Bidirectional Distillation | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Creates a stronger teacher inside the model by raising the number of activated experts per token: first recovers and strengthens the high-activation version, then distills its capability back into the low-tier configuration with the original activation budget. Result: Gains up to 22.2 domain-average points, 6.5 points above RLVR at the same budget, and the low-tier model often surpasses its expanded teacher. | - |
| [2610.23744](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23744) | AlphaLedger: Portfolio-Grounded On-Policy Distillation for Regime-Aware Alpha Mining | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Factor mining: a frozen teacher, conditioned on completed factors and their cost-adjusted marginal portfolio contributions, distills the distribution over legal actions along the student's own trajectories, so deployment no longer needs the teacher. Result: On A-shares, annualized net return 55.4%, Sharpe 2.06 and maximum drawdown 10.8%. | - |
| [2610.26397](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26397) | Bridged Self-Distillation for On-Policy Learning [code](https://github.com/TUMLegalTech/Bridged-Self-Distillation) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | The external teacher only supplies reasoning traces as privileged context; a conditioned copy of the student scores the student's rollouts in the student's own vocabulary and repairs failed trajectories accordingly. Result: Outperforms the respective customized baselines in all three teacher settings: same vocabulary, cross vocabulary, and closed-source API. | - |
| [2610.26740](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26740) | CheckDistill: Adaptive Checklist Distillation for Efficient Distributional Reward Modeling in Image Editing | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | An image editing reward model: it decomposes the editing condition into a checklist of verifiable atoms, a privileged teacher supervises the student's own scoring traces against the checklist, and score tokens are weighted by agreement with the reference labels. Result: Reaches 72.30%, 56.67% and 82.90% on MMRB2, MER-Bench and EditReward-Bench respectively. | - |
