| [2608.12307](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12307) | AI4AI at Test-Time: Strong-to-Weak Capability Transfer via Harnesses | General LLM | External white-box | Without updating parameters, a stronger builder model iteratively constructs an inference-time harness (turning unstable reasoning into code, task routing, format constraints) to transfer capability to a weaker model. Result: Average target-model performance on four theory-of-mind benchmarks rises from 0.49 to 0.91. | - |
| [2608.22788](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22788) | TailSieve: Partial-Rollout-Guided Tail Routing for LLM Rollouts | General LLM | - | Long-tail routing for large-scale RL/OPD rollouts: identifies long-tail groups from partial rollouts without training, hierarchically adjusts the number of isolated groups and the replica partition, and keeps generation on-policy. Result: Routing alone gives up to a 1.67× speedup, and adding routing-specific speculative decoding gives up to 2.59×. | - |
| [2608.29662](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29662) | ACTD: Anchor-Based Cross-Tokenizer Distillation with Residual Regularization | General LLM | External white-box | Cross-vocabulary distillation: first aligns vocabularies and sequences, then suppresses alignment noise with an anchor loss with residual regularization, and extends to multiple teachers; the abstract does not mention student self-sampled trajectories. Result: Best on five reasoning benchmarks with three teachers, and the multi-teacher version beats the strongest single-teacher and multi-teacher baselines. | C |
| [2609.05111](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05111) | Unifying ICL, SFT, KL-Regularized RL Through a Bayesian Lens | General LLM | - | A Bayesian view unifies SFT, few-shot ICL, KL-regularized RL and on-policy distillation into a two-step template that first builds a Gibbs posterior and then takes a forward KL projection. Result: Budget-matched experiments on Qwen3 show that operators with the same learning-signal granularity give almost identical updates under good support and diverge as support degrades. | - |
| [2609.07103](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07103) | Revisiting Complete Reasoning Traces for Post-Training [code](https://github.com/naver-ai/revisiting-trace) | General LLM | - | Questions whether post-training needs complete reasoning traces: analysis shows intermediate tokens contribute little, and training on endpoints alone can still change reasoning behavior and benefit RL and OPD. Result: Partial traces remain effective under heavy truncation, and complete traces bring limited benefit. | N |
| [2609.12419](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12419) | MInTRL: Off-policy Intervention can boost On-policy RL | General LLM | External white-box | Inserts sparse local interventions into rollouts that remain largely on-policy: a judge periodically reviews the output, replaces an incorrect suffix with a short correction and hands control back to the policy, while training uses sequence-level advantage regression to avoid importance sampling. Result: Better than standard on-policy and off-policy baselines on math and code benchmarks; self-intervention also works, and moderate intervention strength is best. | C |
| [2609.13997](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13997) | Unlocking the Unsolvable: Teacher-Guided Curriculum for Data-Efficient RLVR | General LLM | External white-box | Partial reasoning traces from a stronger model create graded difficulty for problems that always fail, a reverse-chain curriculum gradually withdraws the guidance until the model solves them alone, and a monotone frontier curriculum eases the cost of distribution drift. Result: Just 128 originally unsolvable problems match or beat full GRPO on 2000 problems, about 16× the data efficiency. | C |
| [2609.26355](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26355) | PACT: From Credit Assignment to Critic Alignment | General LLM | - | Proves that three regularity conditions uniquely determine token-level credit, making the ideal OPD teacher an implicit critic, and proposes an actor-then-critic training order. Result: Average score of 72.87% across four agentic math benchmarks, 8.80/13.16pp above GRPO/PPO. | - |
| [2609.33426](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33426) | TeacherGRPO: Closing the Capacity Gap in Reasoning Distillation via Teacher Alignment [code](https://github.com/LzyFischer/TeacherGRPO) | General LLM | External white-box | Targets the teacher-student capacity gap by using GRPO to align the teacher's distribution toward the student (curriculum-style selective alignment + importance-adaptive length regularization); the aligned teacher then goes through the standard distillation pipeline. Result: Significantly beats baselines across multiple reasoning benchmarks and distillation methods. | - |
| [2609.35954](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35954) | ROSS: Relearning from Self-Generated Rollouts through Selective Supervision [code](https://github.com/AllSpark-Research/ROSS) | General LLM | Own earlier checkpoint | Treats historical self-sampled trajectories from RL and OPD as reusable experience, keeping full trajectories as context but running offline SFT only on selected continuation segments. Result: On 35B-A3B, the MOPD mean goes 58.40→62.20 and SWE-bench 64.20→68.40. | - |
| [2609.36262](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36262) | Understanding LLM Parameter Update Sparsity through the Lens of Fisher | General LLM | - | Uses the diagonal model Fisher to give a unified explanation of the common cause of sparse parameter updates in RL / OPD / near-policy SFT, and tests the effect of fixed sparse masks. Result: A fixed sparse mask selected by the initial Fisher retains most of the gains of full training. | - |
| [2609.36734](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36734) | Distilling What Matters: Confidence-Aware Selective Distillation for Large Language Models | General LLM | External white-box | A token-level loss that adaptively switches between forward and reverse KL by teacher-student confidence, and rejects updates at the batch level when the teacher is more uncertain than the student; standard KD over a fixed output distribution. Result: Across eight teacher-student pairs and eleven benchmarks, beats strong baselines, with up to +3.2 instruction-following ROUGE-L and +2.1 on MBPP. | - |
| [2609.37041](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37041) | Beam Search as Test-Time Self-Distillation via Counterfactual Contexts | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Moves the self-distillation reward to decoding time: uses the log-odds ratio of good/bad counterfactual contexts as the reward, derives the KL-regularized optimal policy, and approximates it with beam search. Result: On MATH500, HumanEval, and GPQA, outperforms standard sampling, low temperature, and beam search on average. | - |
| [2609.37066](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37066) | Beyond Compression: Diagnosing How Post-Training Changes Mathematical Reasoning | General LLM | External white-box | Uses diagnostics such as cross-surface pass@K to compare off-policy distillation, off-policy-plus-on-policy distillation endpoints, and GRPO, with OPD serving only as one of the released endpoints being compared. Result: On AMC, post-training mainly compresses sampling cost; on AIME it expands the large-K ceiling; GRPO does not beat sufficient off-policy distillation at large K. | - |
| [2609.38987](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38987) | Smaller Models, Better Rejects: Preference Distillation Scaling | General LLM | External white-box | Preference-distillation negatives need not come from the student's own samples: for 7B-72B students, using a smaller frozen model to generate negatives trains stronger students, explained via a DPO utility bound under a linearized feature model. Result: On code and math tasks, small-model negatives beat self-generated negatives, with higher mixing ratios better and lower-likelihood candidate selection preferable. | - |
| [2609.39740](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39740) | LatentHarness: Learning Latent Actions for Memory and Reasoning via Counterfactual Policy Distillation | General LLM | - | Unifies long-context memory and latent reasoning as THINK/RECALL/EXIT latent actions, trained by counterfactual branch scoring; no teacher supervision, only nominally similar to OPD. Result: A 1.4B model improves over the strongest baselines by 2.8% and 10.0% relative on six general and long-context benchmarks, and runs 5.9× faster than the strongest long-context baseline. | - |
| [2610.00903](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00903) | Sharpen Before You Adapt: Data-Free Entry-State Sharpening for Test-Time Reinforcement Learning | General LLM | - | Studies the entry state before test-time RL: data-free training first tunes the checkpoint to lower entropy so subsequent label-free adaptation is more efficient, with self-distillation just one sharpening method. Result: Entry entropy strongly correlates with conversion efficiency (rho=-0.90); SPIRAL drops to 0.07 nats and is best on MATH and GPQA. | - |
| [2610.02140](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02140) | Finetuning with Sampling: SFT Learns Better Than You Think | General LLM | - | Uses MCMC to progressively transform off-policy expert traces into an on-policy distribution closer to the reference model, then does ordinary SFT without changing the learning objective. Result: On scientific skills, mathematical reasoning, and open-ended tasks, rivals on-policy baselines while generalizing better and forgetting less. | NX |
| ★[2610.05872](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05872) | Off-Policy Merging Beats On-Policy Self-Distillation for Continual Learning | General LLM | - | For continual learning, replaces on-policy self-distillation with grafting (learning the update on an earlier checkpoint, applying it after scaling, and masking sensitive directions). Result: Pareto-dominates SFT and OPSD in three settings: expert-trajectory distillation, STaR self-improvement and knowledge injection. | - |
| [2610.09665](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09665) | SAPD: Step-Aligned Privileged Distillation [code](https://github.com/Miaow-Lab/SAPD) | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Fully off-policy, rollout-free privileged self-distillation: aligns each reasoning transition to targeted privileged guidance following the reasoning progress of the reference solution, turning fixed demonstrations into step-level distributional supervision. Result: On math benchmarks it beats SFT and label smoothing on average and matches on-policy RL and self-distillation, with a training loop about 2× faster. | N |
| [2610.10536](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10536) | Decoupling Exploration from Optimization in RLVR [code](https://github.com/SaifPunjwani/Exploration-Distillation) | General LLM | Own earlier checkpoint | First runs trajectories with an exploration policy that has a novelty reward, filters them by correctness and distills them into a separate student policy without the novelty reward, alternating exploration and optimization over multiple rounds. Result: On seven math benchmarks and two model families, it beats DAPO at the same wall-clock budget, with better pass@k scaling. | - |
| [2610.13113](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13113) | Continually Post-training Small Models via Synthetic Self-distillation | General LLM | Weak teacher | Continual post-training for small models: a slightly larger model from the same family does the in-distribution rewriting in place of the small model, paired with off-policy supervision and rejection sampling, explicitly taking the off-policy route. | [2610.15589](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15589) | The Gradient That Top-k Distillation Never Computes [code](https://github.com/KurbanIntelligenceLab/top-k-distillation) | General LLM | External white-box | Proves that after renormalization the top-k distillation objective depends only on log-odds among the retained tokens, and the gradient along the direction that controls tail mass is exactly zero; the full-vocabulary gradient equals the truncated gradient plus a residual term weighted by tail mass. Result: On seven open-source teachers, the distribution can be recovered exactly from k=d+1 (d being the hidden dimension) onward, while commonly cached k values are below this threshold; experiments are off-policy. | - |
| [2610.18878](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18878) | Inverse Reasoning Models for Efficient Post-Training and Verification from Answer-Only Data | General LLM | Same model, privileged | Trains an inverse reasoning model that infers CoT backward from the answer and generates data offline in batches for self-distillation, used for post-training with only question-answer pairs and doubling as a verifier. Result: After GRPO saturates, one round of offline distillation roughly doubles pass@1 on hard problems; verification selection accuracy 0.753→0.969. | - |
| [2610.20057](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20057) | Beyond Correctness: Compute-Responsive Data Selection for Reasoning Model Post-Training | General LLM | - | Proposes compute-responsive data selection, which prioritizes for post-training the problems that flip from wrong to right only when the reasoning budget is increased, and states that this pattern also holds under on-policy distillation. Result: On Qwen3-8B, SFT on CR samples recovers 45.3% of low-budget failures, higher than 40.6% for already-solved samples. | - |
| [2610.22293](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22293) | Towards Continual Learning: Mitigating Forgetting With Subspace Tuning | General LLM | - | Restricts fine-tuning updates to the trailing singular subspace of weight matrices to mitigate forgetting, and can be combined with SFT and self-distillation; OPD serves only as a low-forgetting comparison for motivation. Result: Target-task performance is maintained even with up to 99% of the subspace protected, and KL drift is 2–4 times smaller than the baseline. | - |
| [2610.41104](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41104) | MUON-RT Reusing the Teacher's Trajectory for Momentum-Free Muon for LLM Self-Distillation | General LLM | Own earlier checkpoint | An optimizer improvement for self-distillation training: it reuses the displacement between the EMA teacher and the student in place of Muon's momentum buffer, removes the radial component, and mixes it with the current gradient after spectral normalization. Result: On SDFT it gains +2.6 points over AdamW with 42% less persistent GPU memory, and +5.4 points over momentum Muon with 27% less. | - |

| [2610.02740](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02740) | Prospective Hindsight: Self-Calibrating Reinforcement Learning via Prediction-Reality Gaps | Agents | - | Uses the gap between an agent's pre-action prediction and its post-hoc evaluation as a surprise score and upweights the advantages of these samples, so calibration emerges naturally from optimization. Result: Performance and calibration improve together on single-turn verifiable tasks and multi-turn personal-assistant tasks, consistently across model scales. | - |
| [2610.05195](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05195) | Long-MDR: Long-Context Reinforcement Learning for Multimodal Deep-Research Agents [code](https://github.com/navileon/Long-MDR) | Agents | External white-box | Pushes online RL for multimodal deep-research agents to 128k context and 75+ tool turns, stabilizing training with on-policy distillation warm-up, progressive horizon extension and entropy-triggered rescue. Result: Claims to be the first online multimodal deep-research RL trained at 128k context and 75 tool turns. | - |
| [2610.14000](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14000) | VH-GPO: Verified Handoff for LLM Agent Reinforcement Learning | Agents | External white-box | When an entire group fails, a frozen teacher takes over from the student's state to make the repair and picks the earliest environment-verified point to hand control back, so successful experience is still generated by the student, with no per-token teacher supervision. Result: On Qwen2.5-1.5B, ALFWorld 59.38%→94.79% and WebShop 71.62%→77.34%. | - |
| [2610.14609](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14609) | EviScene: Beyond "Is It AI?" for Agentic Image Factuality Assessment [code](https://github.com/wangzian828/EVISCENE) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Proposes an image factuality assessment task and benchmark, and trains a multimodal ReAct agent: SFT on teacher trajectories, followed by privileged on-policy self-distillation on its own verified decisions. Result: On a 1527-image test set, Qwen3.5-9B reaches 79.05% balanced accuracy and a 52.26% strict evidence sufficiency rate, 6.71/9.37 points above the same-backbone baseline. | - |
| [2610.17670](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17670) | Cowork-Gym: Synthesizing Interactive Work Environments at Scale | Agents | - | Uses a build-execute-revise loop to synthesize collaborative office environments at scale with stateful user simulators, then post-trains models of several sizes with SFT, RL and on-policy distillation. Result: Synthesizes 64.9K environments; 9B/27B/35B-A3B models beat their base models by 8.7/6.4/4.1 points on average across six agent benchmarks. | - |
| [2610.18082](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18082) | Failure Recovery via Belief Updates and World Modeling for Long-Horizon Agents | Agents | Black-box | A failure-recovery framework for long-horizon agents: it maintains a relational belief state across attempts and uses an implicit world model to evaluate repair hypotheses, then distills these reasoning components into a 9B student via SFT plus on-policy distillation. Result: The overall score on three terminal benchmarks is 5.1 points above Terminus-2 continuation; the distilled 9B student beats the native baseline by 9.8 points, and teacher API cost falls by 54.8%. | - |
| [2610.20261](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20261) | Harness Policy Distillation: Aligning Agent Policies with Runtime Semantics | Agents | Same model, privileged | Turns runtime control semantics into a control document that serves as teacher privileged information for generating executable actions; after harness validation, these are distilled into a deployment policy that does not see the document. Result: On TRAJECT-Bench, Qwen3-8B reaches 22.98% autonomous success and 49.23% runtime compliance, versus 3.09% and 19.90% for the baseline. | - |
| [2610.23194](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23194) | OfficeTown: Generative Organizations for Evolving Agent Intelligence | Agents | - | An organizational work simulation framework and a 406-task benchmark that turn work requests, context and review feedback into executable training resources, then train models with SFT and on-policy distillation. Result: Qwen3.5-9B trained with SFT and OPD improves clearly on external professional-task evaluations including GDPval. | - |
| [2610.31846](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31846) | Don't Forget What You Just Learned: Self-Directed Repair Turns Agent Mistakes Into Lasting Lessons | Agents | Same model, privileged | After a failure the agent reflects on its own trajectory to distill a lesson, immediately distills it into a lightweight low-rank adapter, and stores it in a lesson library that can be routed and reused automatically. Result: Success rate improves by 8.9 to 17.0 percentage points on GAIA, Claw-Eval and Terminal-Bench 2.1, with repeated mistakes markedly reduced. | - |
| [2610.35497](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35497) | When Fine-Grained Credit Assignment Helps and Hurts: Multimodal Agentic Reinforcement Learning through Video Editing [code](https://leizhao7.github.io/videoedit/) | Agents | Same model, privileged | Uses a multi-turn video editing agent as a testbed to analyze when fine-grained credit assignment helps, and reports that OPSD gives almost no benefit and even degrades clearly on long trajectories. Result: On Fine Cut, decayed process rewards give a 45.4% relative improvement over outcome-only GRPO; OPSD is highly sensitive to the choice of privileged information. | - |

### Embodied (17)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |

| [2608.22278](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22278) | DreamMimic: Learning Visuomotor Whole-Body Loco-Manipulation via World Model [code](https://github.com/DreamMimic/DreamMimic) | Embodied | External white-box | World-model-assisted policy distillation from a privileged teacher to a visual student, using predicted latent dynamics for multi-step supervision and dynamically balancing teacher guidance and autonomous exploration based on teacher and student performance scores. Result: Beats strong vision baselines on OMOMO and BEHAVE, and does not expose online privileged state to the student at deployment. | - |
| [2609.20056](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20056) | MAGMA-GEN: Validated Recovery Supervision from Ambiguous Failures via Counterfactual Re-Execution | Embodied | External white-box | A privileged coach hypothesizes an early wrong decision in a failed rollout and proposes a local correction, keeping only candidates that genuinely improve on re-execution under the same conditions as recovery supervision data. Result: Higher task success rate and better recovery than distillation and trajectory-repair baselines in simulation and on real hardware. | - |
| [2609.25363](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25363) | HOTICE: Whole-Body Humanoid Object Transportation in Cluttered Environments | Embodied | Multi-teacher | A whole-body control framework for humanoid transportation in cluttered environments: decoupled potential fields plus two-agent RL, then several privileged teacher policies are distilled into a single student policy, with no student trajectory supervision mentioned. Result: Stable transportation of objects of different shapes in MuJoCo and on a real Unitree G1, generalizing to unseen cluttered scenes. | - |
| [2609.29424](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29424) | Coupled State-Space Modelling, Control, and Policy Distillation for Hybrid Rigid-Pneumatic Manipulators | Embodied | External white-box | Builds a coupled dynamics model for a hybrid rigid-pneumatic manipulator, then uses behavior cloning plus DAgger to distill an overly slow MPC into a small policy that runs in real time. Result: The distilled policy reaches 93-94% of targets with zero collisions and fits inside a 5ms control cycle, and the coupled controller tracks 2.5× more accurately than a decoupled PID. | - |
| [2609.29934](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29934) | Beyond Spatial Benchmarks: From Spatial Reasoning to Navigation [code](https://github.com/ylwhxht/Spatial-Nav) | Embodied | - | Analyzes the disconnect between spatial reasoning benchmarks and navigation performance, builds data for two-stage fine-tuning, and has a teacher with spatial priors produce action preferences that are distilled into the student policy. Result: The 8B model reaches 77.4/35.4 SR/SPL on HM3D-v0.2 with only 45 A100 hours of policy training and 148ms per step. | - |
| [2609.34488](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34488) | FlexLoop: Depth-Elastic Looped Policies for Adaptive Test-Time Computation in Deep RL | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Turns fixed-depth looped RL policies into depth-elastic policies: while keeping the original RL objective, it performs policy distillation between adjacent loop depths, passing decision quality from deeper to shallower depths. Result: Across 30 long-horizon goal-conditioned environments, average loop depth drops by up to 43%, with a 1.34× speedup under stress tests. | - |
| [2609.39304](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39304) | Scale and Selection: What Makes Automatic Harness Evolution Work for Visual-Interface Robot Agents | Embodied | Multi-teacher | Uses an optimizer agent to automatically evolve a robot agent's prompts/tools/rules, with a champion-challenger rule filtering revisions, then uses multi-teacher OPD to distill the rollouts back into a standalone policy. Result: LIBERO held-out success 51% to 67% over 30 rounds; the distilled policy reaches 62.2% on RoboCasa365. | - |
| [2610.00317](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00317) | DriftOPD: Sequence-Level Reverse-KL Distillation for One-Step VLA Policies | Embodied | - | Decomposes the sequence-level reverse KL into a chunk-level term and a future-potential term, and trains a one-step VLA action expert with a one-step drifting objective plus an offline Q critic; no teacher, no rollout. Result: Across various VLA architectures in simulation and real-robot manipulation, generally outperforms existing one-step distillation baselines, with success rates close to multi-step teacher policies. | C |
| [2610.02832](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02832) | FastOPD: On-Policy Distillation for Lightweight VLA Deployment [code](https://fastopd.github.io/) | Embodied | External white-box | Compresses a large VLA into a few-step student policy with single-state teacher supervision via a flow map plus a self-consistency objective; it calls itself on-policy distillation but is closer to few-step flow/consistency distillation. Result: On LIBERO, two-step inference retains 84% of π0.5's performance with 78.1% lower latency; single-step +15.9 points on RoboTwin. | C |
| [2610.11039](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11039) | Rendering-Free Lookahead for Question-Guided Active Vision | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Viewpoint selection for active vision: at training time a privileged teacher renders candidate future views with 3DGS and a frozen VLM computes answerability targets, which are distilled in two stages into a rendering-free student. Result: On 377 unseen-environment test episodes of E3VS-Bench, the average judge score is 43% higher than the direct-action baseline. | - |
| [2610.13232](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13232) | FPD-Drive: Future Policy Distillation for End-to-End Autonomous Driving | Embodied | Same model, privileged | End-to-end driving: a privileged teacher that sees recorded future observations produces multiple high-quality trajectory targets to supervise a vision student, with safety-aware candidate-scoring correction; no teacher is needed at deployment. Result: 94.9 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 and 55.9 EPDMS on v2; the Pro version rises to 95.5/58.1, all SOTA. | - |
| [2610.21984](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21984) | GRID-Planner: Graph-Structured Relational Reward Shaping for Diffusion Planning [code](https://github.com/yangzhaojason/GRID-Planner) | Embodied | - | Diffusion planning for autonomous driving: graph-aware importance weighting and time-indexed relational rewards, combined with an advantage-weighted diffusion objective and on-policy self-distillation, form a return-advantage closed loop. Result: Compared with a pure diffusion baseline, collision rate, lane departure and jerk fall by 35.8%, 60.8% and 27.4%, respectively. | - |
| [2610.24847](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24847) | DiPLO: Self-distilled Diffusion Planning for Loco-manipulation | Embodied | Same model, privileged | Humanoid robots: self-distillation first removes the diffusion planner's dependence on strong task conditions, then diffusion-augmented PPO fine-tuning aligns it with the frozen whole-body tracker. Result: Success rate on pick-and-place carrying tasks is 99%, versus only 45% for the pretrained model. | - |
| [2610.25691](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25691) | ReDOA: Real-World Distillation via Observation Alignment for Image-Based Manipulation Policy Learning [code](https://isri-aist.github.io/redoa-project-page/) | Embodied | External white-box | Runs a state-based teacher trained in simulation on a real robot to collect trajectories, then uses observation alignment to distill its control knowledge into an image-based student, without human demonstrations. Result: On three non-prehensile manipulation tasks, overall success rate and execution efficiency beat imitation learning and in-simulation policy distillation. | - |
| [2610.30478](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30478) | Externalise the Memory: A Pre-Registered Study of Where an Embodied Agent's Experience Should Live [code](https://github.com/TPEmist/AionGenos) | Embodied | External white-box | A pre-registered study: distilling the trajectories of a VLM teacher with retrieval memory into LoRA student weights gives almost no gain, whereas attaching the same frozen retrieval buffer to the student weights improves results markedly. Result: Attaching the retrieval buffer gives +34 percentage points (z=5.15), while distilling into the weights gives only +1 percentage point and is not significant. | - |
| [2610.31010](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31010) | Revisiting Distribution Matching for State-to-Vision Policy Distillation in Laparoscopic Robotic Manipulation | Embodied | External white-box | Distills a vision policy for laparoscopic surgery from a privileged-state policy teacher via distribution matching; the abstract does not state that supervision is given step by step on student trajectories. Result: More stable and consistent than general visual RL on several surgical manipulation tasks, and needs fewer demonstrations than imitation learning. | - |
| [2610.37378](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37378) | VLA-Stem: A Lightweight Safety Mechanism for Vision-Language-Action Models via Reinforcement Learning | Embodied | External white-box | Without finetuning the original VLA, it converts action chunks into a trajectory reference and separately trains an obstacle-avoidance policy that adjusts execution at inference; training uses teacher-student distillation plus curriculum learning, with no teacher supervision on student trajectories. Result: Average collision-free success rate of 52.8% across six constrained simulation settings, versus 23.7% for the strongest safety baseline. | - |

| [2608.07981](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07981) | Distilling Physical Priors into Streaming World Models | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | First injects physical priors into a bidirectional DiT teacher, then distills it into a few-step causal streaming generator, and finally strengthens physical consistency with online RL and temporal credit routing. Result: 18.2% better than the Wan2.1-14B teacher on PhysicsIQ. | C |

Result: Each 1.5s clip takes 2.11s on a single H200, and the three-step world model reaches SOTA on WBench. | C |
| [2609.06490](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06490) | OracleZoom: On-Policy Self-Distillation Inspired Reference-Constrained Recursive Image Super Resolution [code](https://dipta007.github.io/OracleZoom/) | Image, video and audio generation | Same model, privileged | Recursive super-resolution trains on its own upscaling trajectory, carrying the last piece of ground-truth evidence across the supervision boundary for direct and cross-scale constraints, plus a no-reference quality objective, a KL prior constraint and EMA consistency. Result: Reaches SOTA across upscaling factors on seven datasets with an average CLIPIQA of 0.713, with larger gains at deeper scales and significantly fewer hallucinations. | J |
| [2609.09123](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09123) | Mask Forcing: Improving Autoregressive Video Diffusion Distillation via Dual-Noise Masking Rollout [code](https://github.com/delaprada/Mask-Forcing) | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | In DMD distillation of autoregressive video diffusion, dual-noise masking injects a cleaner signal into the student's self-rollout input, easing reverse KL mode collapse and reducing error accumulation. Result: Improves visual quality across several autoregressive video diffusion distillation methods without needing real video data or an extra post-training stage. | C |
| [2609.11265](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11265) | Uncertainty DMD: Restoring Diversity in Few-Step Autoregressive Video Distillation | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Analyzes diversity collapse in DMD-distilled autoregressive video generators: the loss of randomness in the first block propagates globally through the deterministic AR cache, and uncertainty is injected via timestep perturbation and stochastic cache writes. Result: Consistent gains in diversity and motion dynamics while single-sample visual quality stays comparable. | C |
| [2609.31349](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31349) | DyMD: Preserving Interaction Dynamics through Distribution Matching Distillation in Few-Step Video World Models | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Improves video DMD: adaptively sets the re-noising timestep by each rollout's interaction fidelity, and gives rollouts with stronger actions more weight when fitting the fake score. Result: Distills a 14B teacher into a four-step 1.3B student, with R-Bench task adherence 9.6 percentage points above Base DMD. | C |
| [2609.32175](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32175) | OneFixer: High-Quality and Consistent One-Step Autoregressive 3DGS Refinement for Driving Scenes [code](https://onefixer-web.vercel.app/) | Image, video and audio generation | - | One-step autoregressive video diffusion that repairs 3DGS renderings, doing flow matching with the model's own one-step predictions as causal context plus pixel-level supervision; it explicitly does not use teacher-student distillation. Result: Lowest FVD/LPIPS/DISTS over 900-frame rollouts, and the closed-loop simulation collision rate is one third lower than with raw 3DGS renderings. | - |
| [2609.35189](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35189) | G³-LoRA: Organizing Reward-Weighted Video Data with Gradient-Guided Grouped LoRA | Image, video and audio generation | Multi-teacher | Groups reward-weighted video data by per-category gradient compatibility to train LoRA experts, then merges them and finishes with on-policy distillation from the experts; OPD is only the last step. Result: On Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B it beats joint reward-weighted LoRA and random/semantic/gradient partitions; distillation recovers part of the merging loss. | J |
| [2609.40037](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40037) | Enhancing Autoregressive Video Generation via Representation Adversarial Distillation | Image, video and audio generation | External white-box | Beyond on-policy DMD for autoregressive video generation, sparsely decodes frames from student rollouts and applies real-data adversarial supervision in the feature space of a frozen visual foundation model. Result: Four-step generation reaches VBench 0.8444 and VideoAlign 0.8033; VBench-Long rises to 0.8041. | C |
| [2610.13944](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13944) | InstantFusion: A Shared Latent Interface for Heterogeneous Image Generators [code](https://github.com/InternLM/InstantFusion) | Image, video and audio generation | - | Trains a shared latent-space interface for heterogeneous diffusion/flow image generators so denoising trajectories can switch between frozen models; cross-architecture on-policy distillation is only one of several downstream applications it showcases. | - |

Result: Trained on only 4 chip cases, it beats OpenEvolve/ShinkaEvolve on average over 16 cases; kernels average 8.27× speedup. | - |

Result: Beats 8 types of baselines on three datasets at path lengths 5/10/20; performance is determined by expert quality rather than the imitation objective. | - |

Result: After seven iterations, Elo rises from 1782 to 2697, and playing strength and puzzle accuracy exceed frontier models. | N |
| [2610.08781](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08781) | IdeaAnchor: Teaching LLMs to Turn Literature into Research Ideas | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Research idea generation: mines structured specifications of "how input literature is synthesized into an idea" from published papers as a privileged signal, then trains the model through demonstrations, self-distillation and RL, with retrieval added at inference. Result: Idea quality improves consistently; anchor training strengthens creative synthesis, and retrieval strengthens detailed elaboration. | - |
| [2610.11111](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11111) | Lapras: Latent Reasoning for Time Series Language Models | Other applications | Same model, privileged | Latent reasoning for time-series language models: the explicit-CoT teacher and the latent-reasoning student share the same origin, and the student aligns with the teacher's hidden states in the answer phase, compressing reasoning ability into continuous thought. Result: Across four backbones and five time-series QA benchmarks, average F1 improves by up to 10.79%, with 23.9× fewer generated tokens. | N |
| [2610.22797](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22797) | Reasoning Reranking with Semantic IDs as the Sole Item Representation [code](https://github.com/lilv98/ReRankID) | Other applications | - | Builds a controlled testbed for reranking reasoning based only on semantic IDs, and compares four post-training methods: SFT, rejection-sampling self-training, on-policy distillation and verifier RL. Result: Trained with a graded ranking reward, the 8B model matches text-reading LLMs whose prompts are about 20 times longer. | - |

Result: VideoEcho-R1 improves consistently across three multimodal backbones and five benchmarks. | C |
| [2609.10321](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10321) | On-Policy Distillation for Vision-Language Model Adaptation, an Effective Paradigm on Low-Quality Multimodal Data | Multimodal understanding | External white-box | Treats construction of the distillation target in vision-language model adaptation as a policy decision, training a lightweight controller that dynamically blends teacher supervision, zero-shot priors and hard labels from reliability and disagreement cues, updated by validation feedback. Result: Consistently better than strong vision-language distillation baselines on base-to-novel generalization and cross-dataset transfer benchmarks. | - |

Result: Target-task gain of 56.9 points (SFT 52.9), old-task loss of 16.6 → 1.5 points, and 11.3× less forgetting. | CJ |

Result: 86.20% accuracy on Cholec80, +9.45 points over the vision-only Qwen3-VL-4B baseline. | - |
| [2609.24244](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24244) | Look Where It Counts: A Free, Label-Free Visual Evidence Signal for Fine-Grained Vision-Language Reasoning | Multimodal understanding | Same model, privileged | Proposes an annotation-free contrastive evidence gap signal whose main result is training-free selection of the region containing the answer; turning the same signal into gated self-distillation training does not beat the base model, reported as an open negative result. Result: Training-free crop selection raises fine-grained accuracy from 70% to 85% and localizes 4.4-5.1× better than random, while SEG-Distill gives no gain. | - |

Result: 55.7% on PanoVideoBench, +12.5 over the backbone and +2.6 over the previous SOTA. | - |

### Safety and privacy (5)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |

| [2608.06526](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06526) | GRASP: Reinforcing Language Model Anonymizers with Group Relative Policy Optimization | Safety and privacy | - | A single small model acts as rewriter, attacker and utility judge at once, training a local anonymizer online with self-generated GRPO rewards in place of DPO distillation. Result: Better than the DPO distillation baseline at about 1% of the cost of a GPT-4o teacher. | - |
| [2608.20554](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20554) | aiXamine: Unified Black-Box Evaluation of Cross-Dimensional Trade-offs in LLM Safety, Security, and Privacy | Safety and privacy | - | A platform for unified black-box evaluation of LLM safety, attack and defense, and privacy, which also characterizes how off-policy distillation without on-policy correction leads to entropy collapse and robustness breakdown. Result: On the same base model, off-policy distillation makes robustness collapse from 56.9 to 2.6; covers 120+ models and 5000+ tests. | - |
| [2610.09462](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09462) | TMT: Runtime Backdoor Detection for Vision-Language-Action Policies on Unseen Tasks [code](https://zzr42.github.io/tmt/) | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | A runtime backdoor detection method for VLAs (token manifold plus latent layer-transition modeling); it also explores purification by supervising a student with the frozen backdoored policy's actions on clean inputs, with distillation only a minor part. Result: On unseen tasks under three types of VLA backdoor attacks, detection performance beats ten adapted baselines. | - |
| [2610.31733](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31733) | Learning to Be Monitored: Causal Bounds and Intervention-Aware Post-Training Recipes for Chain-of-Thought Monitorability [code](https://github.com/xinyue-lxy/learning-to-be-monitored) | Safety and privacy | Same model, privileged | Builds an evaluation framework with upper and lower bounds on the causal detection rate for CoT monitorability, and trains models with three recipes: off-policy self-distillation, RL and on-policy self-distillation. Result: All three methods broadly improve monitorability across a range of model-monitor configurations. | - |
| [2610.33180](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33180) | Propensities Please: Exposing Alignment-Faking LLMs using Synthetic Document Finetuning [code](https://github.com/ardyh/propensities-please) | Safety and privacy | Own earlier checkpoint | Uses synthetic document finetuning to implant cues that expose the latent propensities of alignment-faking models, and uses on-policy distillation to mitigate the reasoning damage caused by that finetuning. Result: Finetuning consistently widens the gap in misaligned behavior rate between prompts with and without the cue, and is more reliable than prompting alone. | - |

### Diffusion language models (3)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [2608.01717](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01717) | Beyond On-Policy Exploration: Integrating External Policy Rollouts for Reinforcement Learning in Diffusion Language Models | Diffusion language models | External white-box | Mixes high-reward rollouts from a stronger external policy into RL for diffusion language models, controlling their length and processing rewards separately by source; there is no teacher supervision on student trajectories. Result: 98.4% best-of-4 completion accuracy on Sudoku, against 40.3% for the strongest baseline. | C |
| [2608.22898](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22898) | SelFusion: Self-distillation for Diffusion Language Models [code](https://github.com/scai-research/SelFusion_official) | Diffusion language models | Same model, privileged | Self-distillation without an external teacher for diffusion language models: two forward passes of the same model at high/low mask rates form hard and easy modes, and per-token correctness dynamically decides the direction of bidirectional KD. Result: Beats KD with external LLM/DLM teachers on instruction-following tasks, and in several configurations the student even surpasses the LLM teacher. | C |
| [2609.40235](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.40235) | Distribution Matching Distillation for Continuous Diffusion Language Models | Diffusion language models | External white-box | Does distribution matching distillation for continuous diffusion language models, giving two few-step methods: Simplex-DMD (relaxation plus pathwise gradients) and Reinforce-DMD (sampling plus a density ratio). Result: On OpenWebText, 4-NFE generation reaches a perplexity of 45.6 (entropy 5.44), and 256 NFE reaches 14.9. | C |

### Quantization and pruning (2)

| arXiv | Title | Domain | Teacher | What it does | Lists |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [2609.11412](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11412) | X-AuT: Progressive Audio-Encoder Compression for Speech LLMs with Cross-Scale Distillation [code](https://xpeng-ai.github.io/x-aut) | Quantization and pruning | External white-box | Progressive pruning of the audio encoder in speech LLMs: short behavioral probes pick the layer combination, then representation alignment, cross-scale distillation, scheduled student-policy supervision and LoRA fine-tuning restore performance. Result: After pruning to 16 layers the macro-average error rate goes 5.61% → 5.27%, against 5.55% for the 1.7B teacher and 8.45% for self-distillation. | - |
| [2610.15398](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15398) | GA-SVD: Generation-aware Singular Value Decomposition for LLM Compression | Quantization and pruning | External white-box | A low-rank compression framework: gradients from on-policy knowledge distillation build a generation-oriented whitening transform, and evolutionary search then performs global layer-wise rank allocation. Result: Outperforms existing SVD baselines on Mistral-7B, the Qwen3 series and Llama-3.3, and scales to 70B. | - |

| [2608.04962](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04962) | SPARK: Learning from Verification in Speculative GRPO Rollouts | Speculative decoding | External white-box | In GRPO rollouts accelerated by speculative decoding, uses the target model's verification feedback to refine the draft model's predictions during generation.
